<p><b>Ghana</b> (<span class="rt-commentedText nowrap"><span class="IPA nopopups noexcerpt" lang="en-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/English" title="Help:IPA/English">/<span style="border-bottom:1px dotted"><span title="/ˈ/: primary stress follows">ˈ</span><span title="/ɡ/: &#39;g&#39; in &#39;guy&#39;">ɡ</span><span title="/ɑː/: &#39;a&#39; in &#39;father&#39;">ɑː</span><span title="&#39;n&#39; in &#39;nigh&#39;">n</span><span title="/ə/: &#39;a&#39; in &#39;about&#39;">ə</span></span>/</a></span>&#32;<span data-nosnippet="" id="ooui-php-1" class="ext-phonos ext-phonos-PhonosButton noexcerpt ext-phonos-PhonosButton-emptylabel oo-ui-widget oo-ui-widget-enabled oo-ui-buttonElement oo-ui-buttonElement-frameless oo-ui-iconElement oo-ui-buttonWidget" data-ooui="{&quot;_&quot;:&quot;mw.Phonos.PhonosButton&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;\/\/upload.wikimedia.org\/wikipedia\/commons\/transcoded\/e\/e4\/En-us-Ghana.ogg\/En-us-Ghana.ogg.mp3&quot;,&quot;rel&quot;:[&quot;nofollow&quot;],&quot;framed&quot;:false,&quot;icon&quot;:&quot;volumeUp&quot;,&quot;data&quot;:{&quot;ipa&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;text&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;lang&quot;:&quot;en&quot;,&quot;wikibase&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;file&quot;:&quot;En-us-Ghana.ogg&quot;},&quot;classes&quot;:[&quot;ext-phonos&quot;,&quot;ext-phonos-PhonosButton&quot;,&quot;noexcerpt&quot;,&quot;ext-phonos-PhonosButton-emptylabel&quot;]}"><a role="button" tabindex="0" href="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/transcoded/e/e4/En-us-Ghana.ogg/En-us-Ghana.ogg.mp3" rel="nofollow" aria-label="Play audio" class="oo-ui-buttonElement-button"><span class="oo-ui-iconElement-icon oo-ui-icon-volumeUp"></span><span class="oo-ui-labelElement-label"></span><span class="oo-ui-indicatorElement-indicator oo-ui-indicatorElement-noIndicator"></span></a></span><sup class="ext-phonos-attribution noexcerpt"><a href="/wiki/File:En-us-Ghana.ogg" title="File:En-us-Ghana.ogg">i</a></sup></span> <a href="/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key" title="Help:Pronunciation respelling key"><i title="English pronunciation respelling"><span style="font-size:90%">GAH</span>-nə</i></a>; <a href="/wiki/Twi_language" class="mw-redirect" title="Twi language">Twi</a>: <i lang="tw">Gaana</i>, <a href="/wiki/Ewe_language" title="Ewe language">Ewe</a>: <i lang="ee">Gana</i>), officially the <b>Republic of Ghana</b>, is a country in <a href="/wiki/West_Africa" title="West Africa">West Africa</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-9" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-9">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup> It abuts the <a href="/wiki/Gulf_of_Guinea" title="Gulf of Guinea">Gulf of Guinea</a> and the Atlantic Ocean to the south, sharing borders with <a href="/wiki/Ivory_Coast" title="Ivory Coast">Ivory Coast</a> <a href="/wiki/Ghana%E2%80%93Ivory_Coast_border" title="Ghana–Ivory Coast border">in the west</a>, <a href="/wiki/Burkina_Faso" title="Burkina Faso">Burkina Faso</a> <a href="/wiki/Burkina_Faso%E2%80%93Ghana_border" title="Burkina Faso–Ghana border">in the north</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Togo" title="Togo">Togo</a> <a href="/wiki/Ghana%E2%80%93Togo_border" title="Ghana–Togo border">in the east</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-warriorking_10-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-warriorking-10">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup> Ghana covers an area of 239,535&#160;km<sup>2</sup> (92,485&#160;sq&#160;mi),<sup id="cite_ref-11" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-11">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup> spanning diverse biomes that range from coastal savannas to tropical rainforests. With over 32 million inhabitants, Ghana is the <a href="/wiki/List_of_African_countries_by_population" title="List of African countries by population">second-most populous country</a> in <a href="/wiki/West_Africa" title="West Africa">West Africa</a>, after <a href="/wiki/Nigeria" title="Nigeria">Nigeria</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-12" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-12">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup> The capital and <a href="/wiki/List_of_cities_in_Ghana" title="List of cities in Ghana">largest city</a> is <a href="/wiki/Accra" title="Accra">Accra</a>; other major cities are <a href="/wiki/Kumasi" title="Kumasi">Kumasi</a>, <a href="/wiki/Tamale,_Ghana" title="Tamale, Ghana">Tamale</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Sekondi-Takoradi" title="Sekondi-Takoradi">Sekondi-Takoradi</a>.
</p><p>The earliest known kingdoms to emerge in modern Ghana were the <a href="/wiki/Mole-Dagbon_people" title="Mole-Dagbon people">Mole-Dagbani</a> states. The <a href="/wiki/Bono_state" title="Bono state">Bono state</a> existed in the area that is modern day Ghana during the 11th century.<sup id="cite_ref-13" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-13">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup> Kingdoms and empires such as <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Dagbon" title="Kingdom of Dagbon">Kingdom of Dagbon</a> in the north<sup id="cite_ref-14" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-14">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup> and the <a href="/wiki/Ashanti_Empire" title="Ashanti Empire">Ashanti Empire</a> in the south emerged over the centuries.<sup id="cite_ref-15" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-15">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup> Beginning in the 15th century, the <a href="/wiki/Portuguese_Empire" title="Portuguese Empire">Portuguese Empire</a>, followed by other <a href="/wiki/European_Powers" class="mw-redirect" title="European Powers">European powers</a>, contested the area for trading rights, until the <a href="/wiki/British_Empire" title="British Empire">British</a> ultimately established control of the coast by the 19th century. Following over a century of colonial resistance, the current borders of the country took shape, encompassing 4 separate British colonial territories: <a href="/wiki/Gold_Coast_(British_colony)" title="Gold Coast (British colony)">Gold Coast</a>, <a href="/wiki/Ashanti_(Crown_Colony)" title="Ashanti (Crown Colony)">Ashanti</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Northern_Territories_of_the_Gold_Coast" title="Northern Territories of the Gold Coast">Northern Territories</a>, and <a href="/wiki/British_Togoland" title="British Togoland">British Togoland</a>. These were unified as an independent dominion within the <a href="/wiki/Commonwealth_of_Nations" title="Commonwealth of Nations">Commonwealth of Nations</a>. On 6th March 1957, Ghana became the first country in <a href="/wiki/Sub-Saharan_Africa" title="Sub-Saharan Africa">Sub-Saharan Africa</a> to achieve sovereignty.<sup id="cite_ref-Universal_Newsreel_16-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Universal_Newsreel-16">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-First_For_Sub-Saharan_Africa_17-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-First_For_Sub-Saharan_Africa-17">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Exploring_Africa_–_Decolinization_18-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Exploring_Africa_–_Decolinization-18">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup> Ghana subsequently became influential in <a href="/wiki/Decolonisation_of_Africa" title="Decolonisation of Africa">decolonisation efforts</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Pan-Africanism" title="Pan-Africanism">Pan-African movement</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Ateku_19-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ateku-19">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Ghana is a multi-ethnic country with linguistic and religious groups;<sup id="cite_ref-popest_20-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-popest-20">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup> while the <a href="/wiki/Akan_people" title="Akan people">Akan</a> are the largest ethnic group, they constitute a plurality. Most <a href="/wiki/Ghanaian_people" class="mw-redirect" title="Ghanaian people">Ghanaians</a> are <a href="/wiki/Christianity_in_Ghana" title="Christianity in Ghana">Christians</a> (71.3%); almost a fifth are <a href="/wiki/Islam_in_Ghana" title="Islam in Ghana">Muslims</a>; a tenth practise traditional faiths or report no religion.<sup id="cite_ref-statsghana1_3-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-statsghana1-3">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup> Ghana is a <a href="/wiki/Unitary_state" title="Unitary state">unitary</a> <a href="/wiki/Liberal_democracy" title="Liberal democracy">constitutional democracy</a> led by a <a href="/wiki/President_of_Ghana" title="President of Ghana">president</a> who is <a href="/wiki/Head_of_state" title="Head of state">head of state</a> and <a href="/wiki/Head_of_government" title="Head of government">head of government</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Ghana:_CIA_World_FactBook_21-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghana:_CIA_World_FactBook-21">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup> For political stability in Africa, Ghana ranked 7th in the 2012 <a href="/wiki/Ibrahim_Index_of_African_Governance" title="Ibrahim Index of African Governance">Ibrahim Index of African Governance</a> and 5th in the 2012 <a href="/wiki/Fragile_States_Index" title="Fragile States Index">Fragile States Index</a>. It has maintained since 1993 one of the freest and most stable governments on the continent, and it performs relatively well in <a href="/wiki/Healthcare_in_Ghana" title="Healthcare in Ghana">healthcare</a>, <a href="/wiki/Economy_of_Ghana" title="Economy of Ghana">economic</a> growth, and human development,<sup id="cite_ref-Ateku_19-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ateku-19">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup> so that it has a significant influence in West Africa and Africa as a whole.<sup id="cite_ref-South_America_and_West_Africa_22-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-South_America_and_West_Africa-22">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup> Ghana is highly integrated in international affairs, being a founding member of the <a href="/wiki/Non-Aligned_Movement" title="Non-Aligned Movement">Non-Aligned Movement</a>, <a href="/wiki/African_Union" title="African Union">African Union</a> and a member of the <a href="/wiki/Economic_Community_of_West_African_States" class="mw-redirect" title="Economic Community of West African States">Economic Community of West African States</a>, <a href="/wiki/Group_of_24" title="Group of 24">Group of 24</a> and <a href="/wiki/Commonwealth_of_Nations" title="Commonwealth of Nations">Commonwealth of Nations</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-23" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-23">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Etymology">Etymology</span></h2>
<p><i>Ghana</i> means "king"<sup id="cite_ref-etymology_24-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-etymology-24">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup> and was the title accorded to the kings of the medieval <a href="/wiki/Ghana_Empire" title="Ghana Empire">Ghana Empire</a> in <a href="/wiki/West_Africa" title="West Africa">West Africa</a>—not to be confused with today's Ghana, for the empire was further north, in modern-day Mali, Senegal and southern Mauritania, as well as in <a href="/wiki/Guinea_(region)" title="Guinea (region)">the region of Guinea</a>.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (March 2023)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>The Republic of Ghana used the word in its name because of "indications that present-day [Akan] inhabitants descended from migrants who moved south from the ancient kingdom of Ghana."<sup id="cite_ref-25" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-25">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="History">History</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/History_of_Ghana" title="History of Ghana">History of Ghana</a></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Medieval_kingdoms">Medieval kingdoms</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Ashanti_Empire" title="Ashanti Empire">Ashanti Empire</a> and <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Dagbon" title="Kingdom of Dagbon">Kingdom of Dagbon</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Guinea_from_Milner%27s_Atlas.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/27/Guinea_from_Milner%27s_Atlas.jpg/220px-Guinea_from_Milner%27s_Atlas.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="156" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/27/Guinea_from_Milner%27s_Atlas.jpg/330px-Guinea_from_Milner%27s_Atlas.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/27/Guinea_from_Milner%27s_Atlas.jpg/440px-Guinea_from_Milner%27s_Atlas.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1008" data-file-height="717" /></a><figcaption>An 1850 map showing the <a href="/wiki/Akan_people" title="Akan people">Akan</a> <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Ashanti" class="mw-redirect" title="Kingdom of Ashanti">Kingdom of Ashanti</a> within the <a href="/wiki/Guinea_(region)" title="Guinea (region)">Guinea region</a> and surrounding regions in West Africa</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Lidded_Vessel_(Kuduo)_MET_DP108293.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a2/Lidded_Vessel_%28Kuduo%29_MET_DP108293.jpg/220px-Lidded_Vessel_%28Kuduo%29_MET_DP108293.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="247" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a2/Lidded_Vessel_%28Kuduo%29_MET_DP108293.jpg/330px-Lidded_Vessel_%28Kuduo%29_MET_DP108293.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a2/Lidded_Vessel_%28Kuduo%29_MET_DP108293.jpg/440px-Lidded_Vessel_%28Kuduo%29_MET_DP108293.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1744" data-file-height="1960" /></a><figcaption>18th-century Ashanti brass <i>kuduo</i>. Gold dust and nuggets were kept in <i>kuduo</i>, as were other items of personal value and significance. As receptacles for their owners' <i>kra</i>, or life force, <i>kuduo</i> were prominent features of ceremonies designed to honour and protect that individual.</figcaption></figure>
<p>The earliest known kingdoms to emerge in modern Ghana were the <a href="/w/index.php?title=Mole-Dagbani&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Mole-Dagbani (page does not exist)">Mole-Dagbani</a> states.<sup id="cite_ref-col_26-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-col-26">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup> The Mole-Dagomba came on horseback from what later became <a href="/wiki/Burkina_Faso" title="Burkina Faso">Burkina Faso</a> under a single leader, <a href="/wiki/Naa_Gbewaa" title="Naa Gbewaa">Naa Gbewaa</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-27" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-27">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup> They invaded and occupied the lands of the local people ruled by the <i>tendamba</i> (land god priests), established themselves as the rulers over the locals, and made <a href="/wiki/Gambaga" title="Gambaga">Gambaga</a> their capital.<sup id="cite_ref-28" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-28">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup> The death of Naa Gbewaa caused seccession among their children, some of whom broke off and founded separate states including <a href="/wiki/Mamprusi_people" title="Mamprusi people">Mamprugu</a> and <a href="/wiki/Nanumba_people" title="Nanumba people">Nanung</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-29" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-29">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-30" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-30">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Akan_people" title="Akan people">Akan</a>-speaking peoples began to move into what later is Ghana toward the 15th century.<sup id="cite_ref-col_26-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-col-26">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-31" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-31">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup> By the 16th century, the Akans were established in the Akan state called <a href="/wiki/Bono_state" title="Bono state">Bonoman</a>, for which the <a href="/wiki/Brong-Ahafo_region" title="Brong-Ahafo region">Brong-Ahafo region</a> was named.<sup id="cite_ref-col_26-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-col-26">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-32" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-32">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup> From the 17th century, Akans emerged from what is believed to have been the Bonoman area, to create Akan states, mainly based on gold trading.<sup id="cite_ref-33" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-33">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup> These states included Bonoman (Brong-Ahafo region), Ashanti (<a href="/wiki/Ashanti_Region" title="Ashanti Region">Ashanti Region</a>), <a href="/wiki/Denkyira" title="Denkyira">Denkyira</a> (<a href="/wiki/Central_Region_(Ghana)" title="Central Region (Ghana)">Western North region</a>), <a href="/wiki/Mankessim_Kingdom" title="Mankessim Kingdom">Mankessim Kingdom</a> (<a href="/wiki/Western_Region_(Ghana)" title="Western Region (Ghana)">Central region</a>), and <a href="/wiki/Akwamu" title="Akwamu">Akwamu</a> <a href="/wiki/Eastern_Region_(Ghana)" title="Eastern Region (Ghana)">(Eastern region)</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-col_26-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-col-26">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup> By the 19th century, the territory of the southern part of Ghana was included in the <a href="/wiki/Ashanti_Empire" title="Ashanti Empire">Kingdom of Ashanti</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-col_26-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-col-26">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup> The government of the Ashanti Empire operated first as a loose network and eventually as a centralised kingdom with a specialised bureaucracy centred in the capital city of <a href="/wiki/Kumasi" title="Kumasi">Kumasi</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-col_26-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-col-26">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup> Prior to Akan contact with Europeans, the Akan people created an economy based on principally gold and <a href="/wiki/Gold_bar" title="Gold bar">gold bar</a> <a href="/wiki/Commodity" title="Commodity">commodities</a>, which were traded with other states in Africa.<sup id="cite_ref-col_26-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-col-26">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-34" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-34">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup>
The Ga-Dangme and Ewe migrated westward from south-western Nigeria. The Ewe migrated from Oyo area with their Gbe-speaking kinsmen (Adja, Fon, Phera Gun)and in transition, settled in Ketou in Benin Republic, Tado in Togo and with Nortsie ( a walled town on present day Togo)as their final dispersal point. Their dispersal from Nortsie was necessitated by the high handed rule of King Agorkorli (Agor Akorlie). The <a href="/wiki/Ga-Adangbe_people" title="Ga-Adangbe people">Ga- Dangme</a> occupy the Greater Accra Region and parts of the Eastern Region, while the Ewe are found in the <a href="/wiki/Volta_Region" title="Volta Region">Volta Region</a> as well as the neighbouring Togo, Benin Republic and Nigeria ( around Badagry area).
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="European_contact_and_colonialism">European contact and colonialism</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Slave_Coast_of_West_Africa" title="Slave Coast of West Africa">Slave Coast of West Africa</a> and <a href="/wiki/Dutch_Slave_Coast" title="Dutch Slave Coast">Dutch Slave Coast</a></div><figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Elmina_slave_castle.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a6/Elmina_slave_castle.jpg/220px-Elmina_slave_castle.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="167" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a6/Elmina_slave_castle.jpg/330px-Elmina_slave_castle.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a6/Elmina_slave_castle.jpg/440px-Elmina_slave_castle.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1152" data-file-height="872" /></a><figcaption>The <a href="/wiki/Portuguese_Empire" title="Portuguese Empire">Portuguese</a> established the <a href="/wiki/Portuguese_Gold_Coast" title="Portuguese Gold Coast">Portuguese Gold Coast</a> with the construction of <a href="/wiki/Elmina_Castle" title="Elmina Castle">Elmina Castle</a> (<i>Castelo da Mina</i>) by <a href="/wiki/Diogo_de_Azambuja" title="Diogo de Azambuja">Diogo de Azambuja</a> in 1482, making it the oldest <a href="/wiki/Portuguese_colonial_architecture" title="Portuguese colonial architecture">European building</a> in <a href="/wiki/Sub-Saharan_Africa" title="Sub-Saharan Africa">sub-Saharan Africa</a>.</figcaption></figure>
<p>Akan trade with European states began after contact with the <a href="/wiki/Portuguese_Empire" title="Portuguese Empire">Portuguese</a> in the 15th century.<sup id="cite_ref-35" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-35">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup> European contact by the <a href="/wiki/Portuguese_people" title="Portuguese people">Portuguese people</a>, who came to the <a href="/wiki/Gold_Coast_(region)" title="Gold Coast (region)">Gold Coast region</a> in the 15th century to trade. The Portuguese then established the <a href="/wiki/Portuguese_Gold_Coast" title="Portuguese Gold Coast">Portuguese Gold Coast</a> (Costa do Ouro), focused on the availability of gold.<sup id="cite_ref-History_36-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-History-36">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup> The Portuguese built a trading lodge at a coastal settlement called Anomansah (the perpetual drink) which they renamed <a href="/wiki/Elmina" title="Elmina">São Jorge da Mina</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-History_36-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-History-36">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup> In 1481, King <a href="/wiki/John_II_of_Portugal" title="John II of Portugal">John II of Portugal</a> commissioned <a href="/wiki/Diogo_de_Azambuja" title="Diogo de Azambuja">Diogo de Azambuja</a> to build the <a href="/wiki/Elmina_Castle" title="Elmina Castle">Elmina Castle</a>, which was completed in 3 years.<sup id="cite_ref-History_36-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-History-36">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup> By 1598, the <a href="/wiki/Dutch_people" title="Dutch people">Dutch</a> had joined the Portuguese in the gold trade, establishing the <a href="/wiki/Dutch_Gold_Coast" title="Dutch Gold Coast">Dutch Gold Coast</a> (<i>Nederlandse Bezittingen ter Kuste van Guinea</i> - 'Dutch properties at the Guinea coast') and building forts at <a href="/wiki/Fort_Komenda" title="Fort Komenda">Fort Komenda</a> and Kormantsi.<sup id="cite_ref-Ghana_book_37-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghana_book-37">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup> In 1617, the Dutch captured the Elmina Castle from the Portuguese and <a href="/wiki/Axim" title="Axim">Axim</a> in 1642 (<a href="/wiki/Fort_Saint_Anthony" title="Fort Saint Anthony">Fort St Anthony</a>).<sup id="cite_ref-Ghana_book_37-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghana_book-37">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>European traders had joined in gold trading by the 17th century, including the <a href="/wiki/Swedes" title="Swedes">Swedes</a>, establishing the <a href="/wiki/Swedish_Gold_Coast" title="Swedish Gold Coast">Swedish Gold Coast</a> (<i>Svenska Guldkusten</i>), and <a href="/wiki/Denmark%E2%80%93Norway" title="Denmark–Norway">Denmark–Norway</a>, establishing the <a href="/wiki/Danish_Gold_Coast" title="Danish Gold Coast">Danish Gold Coast</a> (<i>Danske Guldkyst</i> or <i>Dansk Guinea</i>).<sup id="cite_ref-History_of_Ghana_38-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-History_of_Ghana-38">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup> European traders participated in the <a href="/wiki/Atlantic_slave_trade" title="Atlantic slave trade">Atlantic slave trade</a> in this area.<sup id="cite_ref-Emmer_39-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Emmer-39">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup> More than 30 forts and castles were built by the merchants. The Germans established the <a href="/wiki/Brandenburger_Gold_Coast" title="Brandenburger Gold Coast">Brandenburger Gold Coast</a> or Groß Friedrichsburg).<sup id="cite_ref-40" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-40">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup> In 1874, Great Britain established control over some parts of the country, assigning these areas the status of the <a href="/wiki/Gold_Coast_(British_colony)" title="Gold Coast (British colony)">British Gold Coast</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-colestablish_41-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-colestablish-41">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup> Military engagements occurred between British colonial powers and Akan nation-states. The Kingdom of <a href="/wiki/Ashanti_Empire" title="Ashanti Empire">Ashanti</a> defeated the British some times in the 100-year-long <a href="/wiki/Anglo-Ashanti_wars" title="Anglo-Ashanti wars">Anglo-Ashanti wars</a> and eventually lost with the <a href="/wiki/War_of_the_Golden_Stool" title="War of the Golden Stool">War of the Golden Stool</a> in 1900.<sup id="cite_ref-42" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-42">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Chronology_of_world_history_43-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Chronology_of_world_history-43">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-44" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-44">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Transition_to_independence">Transition to independence</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Dominion_of_Ghana" title="Dominion of Ghana">Dominion of Ghana</a> and <a href="/wiki/Ghana_Independence_Act_1957" title="Ghana Independence Act 1957">Ghana Independence Act 1957</a></div>

marking Ghana's independence on 6 March 1957</div></div></div></div></div>
<p>In 1947, the newly formed <a href="/wiki/United_Gold_Coast_Convention" title="United Gold Coast Convention">United Gold Coast Convention</a> led by <a href="/wiki/The_Big_Six_(Ghana)" title="The Big Six (Ghana)">"The Big Six"</a> called for "self-government within the shortest possible time" following the <a href="/wiki/1946_Gold_Coast_general_election" title="1946 Gold Coast general election">1946 Gold Coast legislative election</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-History_of_Ghana_38-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-History_of_Ghana-38">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-45" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-45">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Kwame_Nkrumah" title="Kwame Nkrumah">Kwame Nkrumah</a>, a Ghanaian nationalist who led Ghana from 1957 to 1966 as the country's first <a href="/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_Ghana" title="Prime Minister of Ghana">prime minister</a> and <a href="/wiki/President_of_Ghana" title="President of Ghana">president</a>, formed the <a href="/wiki/Convention_People%27s_Party" title="Convention People&#39;s Party">Convention People's Party</a> in 1949 with the motto "self-government now".<sup id="cite_ref-History_of_Ghana_38-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-History_of_Ghana-38">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup> The party initiated a "positive action" campaign involving non-violent protests, strikes and non-cooperation with the British authorities. Nkrumah was arrested and sentenced to one year imprisonment during this time. In the Gold Coast's <a href="/wiki/1951_Gold_Coast_general_election" title="1951 Gold Coast general election">1951 general election</a>, he was elected to Parliament and was released from prison.<sup id="cite_ref-History_of_Ghana_38-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-History_of_Ghana-38">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup> he became prime minister in 1952 and began a policy of Africanization.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (March 2023)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>On 6 March 1957 at midnight, the Gold Coast, Ashanti, the Northern Territories, and <a href="/wiki/British_Togoland" title="British Togoland">British Togoland</a> were unified as one single independent dominion within the <a href="/wiki/Commonwealth_of_Nations" title="Commonwealth of Nations">British Commonwealth</a> under the name Ghana. This was done under the <a href="/wiki/Ghana_Independence_Act_1957" title="Ghana Independence Act 1957">Ghana Independence Act 1957</a>. The current <a href="/wiki/Flag_of_Ghana" title="Flag of Ghana">flag of Ghana</a>, consisting of the colours red, gold, green, and a black star, dates back to this unification.<sup id="cite_ref-Ghana_flag_and_description_46-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghana_flag_and_description-46">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup> On 1 July 1960, following the <a href="/wiki/1960_Ghanaian_constitutional_referendum" title="1960 Ghanaian constitutional referendum">Ghanaian constitutional referendum</a> and <a href="/wiki/1960_Ghanaian_presidential_election" title="1960 Ghanaian presidential election">Ghanaian presidential election</a>, Nkrumah declared Ghana a republic and assumed the presidency.<sup id="cite_ref-Universal_Newsreel_16-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Universal_Newsreel-16">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-First_For_Sub-Saharan_Africa_17-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-First_For_Sub-Saharan_Africa-17">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Exploring_Africa_–_Decolinization_18-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Exploring_Africa_–_Decolinization-18">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-History_of_Ghana_38-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-History_of_Ghana-38">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup> 6 March is the nation's <a href="/wiki/Independence_Day_(Ghana)" title="Independence Day (Ghana)">Independence Day</a>, and 1 July is celebrated as <a href="/wiki/Republic_Day" title="Republic Day">Republic Day</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-47" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-47">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-48" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-48">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Nkrumah led an <a href="/wiki/Authoritarianism" title="Authoritarianism">authoritarian</a> regime in Ghana, as he repressed political opposition and conducted elections that were not free and fair.<sup id="cite_ref-49" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-49">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-50" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-50">&#91;50&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-51" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-51">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-52" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-52">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-53" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-53">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup> In 1964, a <a href="/wiki/1964_Ghanaian_constitutional_referendum" title="1964 Ghanaian constitutional referendum">constitutional amendment</a> made Ghana a <a href="/wiki/One-party_state" title="One-party state">one-party state</a>, with Nkrumah as <a href="/wiki/President_for_life" title="President for life">president for life</a> of both the nation and its party.<sup id="cite_ref-54" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-54">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup> Nkrumah was the first African head of state to promote the concept of <a href="/wiki/Pan-Africanism" title="Pan-Africanism">Pan-Africanism</a>, which he had been introduced to during his studies at <a href="/wiki/Lincoln_University_(Pennsylvania)" title="Lincoln University (Pennsylvania)">Lincoln University</a>, Pennsylvania in the United States, at the time when <a href="/wiki/Marcus_Garvey" title="Marcus Garvey">Marcus Garvey</a> was known for his "Back to Africa Movement".<sup id="cite_ref-History_of_Ghana_38-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-History_of_Ghana-38">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup> He merged the teachings of Garvey, <a href="/wiki/Martin_Luther_King_Jr." title="Martin Luther King Jr.">Martin Luther King Jr.</a> and the naturalised Ghanaian scholar <a href="/wiki/W._E._B._Du_Bois" title="W. E. B. Du Bois">W. E. B. Du Bois</a> into the formation of 1960s Ghana.<sup id="cite_ref-History_of_Ghana_38-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-History_of_Ghana-38">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup> Osagyefo Dr. Kwame Nkrumah, as he became known, played an instrumental part in the founding of the <a href="/wiki/Non-Aligned_Movement" title="Non-Aligned Movement">Non-Aligned Movement</a>, and in establishing the <a href="/wiki/Kwame_Nkrumah_Ideological_Institute" title="Kwame Nkrumah Ideological Institute">Kwame Nkrumah Ideological Institute</a> to teach his ideologies of <a href="/wiki/Communism" title="Communism">communism</a> and <a href="/wiki/Socialism" title="Socialism">socialism</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-55" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-55">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup> His life achievements were recognised by Ghanaians during his centenary birthday celebration, and the day was instituted as a <a href="/wiki/Public_holidays_in_Ghana" title="Public holidays in Ghana">public holiday in Ghana</a> (<a href="/wiki/Founders%27_Day_(Ghana)" title="Founders&#39; Day (Ghana)">Founders' Day</a>).<sup id="cite_ref-56" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-56">&#91;56&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Operation_Cold_Chop_and_aftermath">Operation Cold Chop and aftermath</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/History_of_Ghana_(1966%E2%80%931979)" title="History of Ghana (1966–1979)">History of Ghana (1966–1979)</a></div>
<p>The government of Nkrumah was subsequently overthrown in a coup by the <a href="/wiki/Ghana_Armed_Forces" title="Ghana Armed Forces">Ghana Armed Forces</a>, codenamed "Operation Cold Chop". This occurred while Nkrumah was abroad with <a href="/wiki/Zhou_Enlai" title="Zhou Enlai">Zhou Enlai</a> in the People's Republic of China, on a fruitless mission to <a href="/wiki/Hanoi" title="Hanoi">Hanoi</a>, Vietnam, to help end the <a href="/wiki/Vietnam_War" title="Vietnam War">Vietnam War</a>. The coup took place on 24 February 1966, led by Colonel <a href="/wiki/Emmanuel_Kwasi_Kotoka" title="Emmanuel Kwasi Kotoka">Emmanuel Kwasi Kotoka</a> and Brigadier Akwasi Afrifa. The <a href="/wiki/National_Liberation_Council" title="National Liberation Council">National Liberation Council</a> was formed, chaired by Lieutenant General <a href="/wiki/Joseph_Arthur_Ankrah" title="Joseph Arthur Ankrah">Joseph A. Ankrah</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-political_&amp;_social_thought_of_Kwame_Nkrumah_57-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-political_&amp;_social_thought_of_Kwame_Nkrumah-57">&#91;57&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>A series of alternating military and civilian governments, often affected by economic instabilities,<sup id="cite_ref-58" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-58">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup> ruled Ghana from 1966, ending with the ascent to power of <a href="/wiki/Flight_lieutenant" title="Flight lieutenant">Flight Lieutenant</a> <a href="/wiki/Jerry_Rawlings" title="Jerry Rawlings">Jerry John Rawlings</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Provisional_National_Defence_Council" title="Provisional National Defence Council">Provisional National Defence Council</a> in 1981.<sup id="cite_ref-Flight_Lieutenant_Jerry_John_Rawlings_(J.J_Rawlings)_59-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Flight_Lieutenant_Jerry_John_Rawlings_(J.J_Rawlings)-59">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup> These changes resulted in the suspension of the <a href="/wiki/Constitution_of_Ghana" title="Constitution of Ghana">constitution</a> in 1981 and the banning of <a href="/wiki/List_of_political_parties_in_Ghana" title="List of political parties in Ghana">political parties</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Rawlings:_The_legacy_60-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Rawlings:_The_legacy-60">&#91;60&#93;</a></sup> The economy soon declined, so Rawlings negotiated a structural adjustment plan, changing many old economic policies, and growth recovered during the mid-1980s.<sup id="cite_ref-Rawlings:_The_legacy_60-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Rawlings:_The_legacy-60">&#91;60&#93;</a></sup> A new constitution restoring <a href="/wiki/Multi-party_system" title="Multi-party system">multi-party system</a> politics was promulgated in the <a href="/wiki/1992_Ghanaian_presidential_election" title="1992 Ghanaian presidential election">presidential election of 1992</a>, in which Rawlings was elected, and again in the <a href="/wiki/1996_Ghanaian_general_election" title="1996 Ghanaian general election">general election of 1996</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Elections_in_Ghana_61-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Elections_in_Ghana-61">&#91;61&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In a <a href="/wiki/Konkomba%E2%80%93Nanumba_conflict" title="Konkomba–Nanumba conflict">tribal war</a> in <a href="/wiki/Northern_Region_(Ghana)" title="Northern Region (Ghana)">Northern Ghana</a> in 1994, between the <a href="/wiki/Konkomba_people" title="Konkomba people">Konkomba</a> and other ethnic groups, including the <a href="/wiki/Nanumba_people" title="Nanumba people">Nanumba</a>, <a href="/wiki/Dagomba_people" title="Dagomba people">Dagomba</a> and <a href="/wiki/Gonja_people" title="Gonja people">Gonja</a>, between 1,000 and 2,000 people were killed and 150,000 people were displaced.<sup id="cite_ref-62" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-62">&#91;62&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Kpetoee.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c9/Kpetoee.jpg/220px-Kpetoee.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c9/Kpetoee.jpg/330px-Kpetoee.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c9/Kpetoee.jpg/440px-Kpetoee.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1000" data-file-height="667" /></a><figcaption>Traditional <a href="/wiki/Chieftaincy" class="mw-redirect" title="Chieftaincy">chiefs</a> in 2015</figcaption></figure>
<p>After the <a href="/wiki/2000_Ghanaian_general_election" title="2000 Ghanaian general election">2000 general election</a>, <a href="/wiki/John_Kufuor" title="John Kufuor">John Kufuor</a> of the <a href="/wiki/New_Patriotic_Party" title="New Patriotic Party">New Patriotic Party</a> became president of Ghana on 7 January 2001 and was <a href="/wiki/2004_Ghanaian_general_election" title="2004 Ghanaian general election"> re-elected in 2004</a>, thus also serving two terms (the term limit) as president of Ghana and marking the first time under the fourth republic that power was transferred from one legitimately elected head of state and head of government to another.<sup id="cite_ref-Elections_in_Ghana_61-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Elections_in_Ghana-61">&#91;61&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Nana_Akufo-Addo" title="Nana Akufo-Addo">Nana Akufo-Addo</a>, the ruling party candidate, was defeated in a very close <a href="/wiki/2008_Ghanaian_general_election" title="2008 Ghanaian general election">2008 general election</a> by <a href="/wiki/John_Atta_Mills" title="John Atta Mills">John Atta Mills</a> of the <a href="/wiki/National_Democratic_Congress_(Ghana)" title="National Democratic Congress (Ghana)">National Democratic Congress</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-63" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-63">&#91;63&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-64" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-64">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup> Mills died of natural causes and was succeeded by Vice President <a href="/wiki/John_Mahama" title="John Mahama">John Mahama</a> on 24 July 2012.<sup id="cite_ref-65" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-65">&#91;65&#93;</a></sup> Following the <a href="/wiki/2012_Ghanaian_general_election" title="2012 Ghanaian general election">2012 general election</a>, Mahama became president in his own right,<sup id="cite_ref-Ghanaian_President_John_Dramani_Mahama_sworn_in_66-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghanaian_President_John_Dramani_Mahama_sworn_in-66">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup> and Ghana was described as a "stable democracy".<sup id="cite_ref-OBG2012_67-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-OBG2012-67">&#91;67&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-68" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-68">&#91;68&#93;</a></sup> As a result of the <a href="/wiki/2016_Ghanaian_general_election" title="2016 Ghanaian general election">2016 general election</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-69" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-69">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Nana_Akufo-Addo" title="Nana Akufo-Addo">Nana Akufo-Addo</a> became president on 7 January 2017.<sup id="cite_ref-70" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-70">&#91;70&#93;</a></sup> He was re-elected after a tightly contested <a href="/wiki/2020_Ghanaian_general_election" title="2020 Ghanaian general election">election in 2020</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-71" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-71">&#91;71&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>To combat deforestation, on 11 June 2021 Ghana inaugurated Green Ghana Day, with the aim of planting 5 million trees in a concentrated effort to preserve the country's rainforest cover.<sup id="cite_ref-72" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-72">&#91;72&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Geography">Geography</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Geography_of_Ghana" title="Geography of Ghana">Geography of Ghana</a> and <a href="/wiki/Geology_of_Ghana" title="Geology of Ghana">Geology of Ghana</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Biodiversity_of_Ghana" title="Biodiversity of Ghana">Biodiversity of Ghana</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1096954695/mw-parser-output/.tmulti"><div class="thumb tmulti tright"><div class="thumbinner multiimageinner" style="width:322px;max-width:322px"><div class="trow"><div class="tsingle" style="width:176px;max-width:176px"><div class="thumbimage" style="height:203px;overflow:hidden"><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:Ghana_Topography.png" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/12/Ghana_Topography.png/174px-Ghana_Topography.png" decoding="async" width="174" height="204" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/12/Ghana_Topography.png/261px-Ghana_Topography.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/12/Ghana_Topography.png/348px-Ghana_Topography.png 2x" data-file-width="794" data-file-height="931" /></a></span></div><div class="thumbcaption"><a href="/wiki/Topography" title="Topography">Topography</a>−<a href="/wiki/Topographic_map" title="Topographic map">topographic map</a></div></div><div class="tsingle" style="width:142px;max-width:142px"><div class="thumbimage" style="height:203px;overflow:hidden"><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:Ghana_sat.png" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d4/Ghana_sat.png/140px-Ghana_sat.png" decoding="async" width="140" height="203" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d4/Ghana_sat.png/210px-Ghana_sat.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d4/Ghana_sat.png/280px-Ghana_sat.png 2x" data-file-width="534" data-file-height="774" /></a></span></div><div class="thumbcaption"><a href="/wiki/Satellite_imagery" title="Satellite imagery">Satellite image</a></div></div></div></div></div>
<p>Ghana is located on the <a href="/wiki/Gulf_of_Guinea" title="Gulf of Guinea">Gulf of Guinea</a>, a few degrees north of the <a href="/wiki/Equator" title="Equator">Equator</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Geography_Physical_73-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Geography_Physical-73">&#91;73&#93;</a></sup> It spans an area of 239,535&#160;km<sup>2</sup> (92,485&#160;sq&#160;mi)<sup id="cite_ref-74" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-74">&#91;74&#93;</a></sup> and has an Atlantic coastline that stretches 560 kilometres (350 miles) on the Gulf of Guinea in the Atlantic Ocean to its south.<sup id="cite_ref-Geography_Physical_73-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Geography_Physical-73">&#91;73&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Dodi_Island" title="Dodi Island">Dodi Island</a> and <a href="/wiki/Bobowasi_Island" title="Bobowasi Island">Bobowasi Island</a> are near the south coast.<sup id="cite_ref-Ghana_low_plains_75-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghana_low_plains-75">&#91;75&#93;</a></sup> It lies between latitudes 4°45'N and 11°N, and longitudes 1°15'E and 3°15'W. The <a href="/wiki/Prime_meridian" title="Prime meridian">prime meridian</a> passes through Ghana, specifically through <a href="/wiki/Tema" title="Tema">Tema</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Geography_Physical_73-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Geography_Physical-73">&#91;73&#93;</a></sup> Ghana is geographically closer to the "centre" of the Earth than any other country, since the notional centre, (0°, 0°) is located in the Atlantic Ocean approximately 614&#160;km (382&#160;mi) off the south-east coast of Ghana.
</p><p>Grasslands mixed with south coastal shrublands and forests dominate Ghana, with forest extending northward from the coast 320 kilometres (200 miles) and eastward for a maximum of about 270 kilometres (170 miles) with locations for mining of industrial minerals and timber.<sup id="cite_ref-Geography_Physical_73-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Geography_Physical-73">&#91;73&#93;</a></sup> Ghana is home to 5 terrestrial ecoregions: <a href="/wiki/Eastern_Guinean_forests" title="Eastern Guinean forests">Eastern Guinean forests</a>, <a href="/wiki/Guinean_forest%E2%80%93savanna_mosaic" title="Guinean forest–savanna mosaic">Guinean forest–savanna mosaic</a>, <a href="/wiki/West_Sudanian_savanna" title="West Sudanian savanna">West Sudanian savanna</a>, <a href="/wiki/Central_African_mangroves" title="Central African mangroves">Central African mangroves</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Guinean_mangroves" title="Guinean mangroves">Guinean mangroves</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-DinersteinOlson2017_76-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-DinersteinOlson2017-76">&#91;76&#93;</a></sup> It had a 2018 <a href="/wiki/Forest_Landscape_Integrity_Index" title="Forest Landscape Integrity Index">Forest Landscape Integrity Index</a> mean score of 4.53/10, ranking it 112th globally out of 172 countries.<sup id="cite_ref-FLII-Supplementary_77-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FLII-Supplementary-77">&#91;77&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>
The <a href="/wiki/White_Volta" title="White Volta">White Volta</a> River and its tributary <a href="/wiki/Black_Volta" title="Black Volta">Black Volta</a>, flow south through Ghana to <a href="/wiki/Lake_Volta" title="Lake Volta">Lake Volta</a>, the world's <a href="/wiki/List_of_reservoirs_by_volume" title="List of reservoirs by volume">third-largest reservoir by volume</a> and largest by surface area, formed by the hydroelectric <a href="/wiki/Akosombo_Dam" title="Akosombo Dam">Akosombo Dam</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-78" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-78">&#91;78&#93;</a></sup> completed in 1965. The Volta flows out of Lake Volta into the <a href="/wiki/Gulf_of_Guinea" title="Gulf of Guinea">Gulf of Guinea</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-79" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-79">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup> The northernmost part of Ghana is Pulmakong and the southernmost part of Ghana is <a href="/wiki/Cape_Three_Points" title="Cape Three Points">Cape Three Points</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Geography_Physical_73-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Geography_Physical-73">&#91;73&#93;</a></sup></p><div style="clear:both;"></div>

<td rowspan="12"><figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-center" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Ghana_Regions_map.png" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8e/Ghana_Regions_map.png/300px-Ghana_Regions_map.png" decoding="async" width="300" height="448" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8e/Ghana_Regions_map.png/450px-Ghana_Regions_map.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8e/Ghana_Regions_map.png/600px-Ghana_Regions_map.png 2x" data-file-width="1495" data-file-height="2233" /></a><figcaption><div class="center" style="width:auto; margin-left:auto; margin-right:auto;">Map with <a href="/wiki/National_border" class="mw-redirect" title="National border">national border</a>, <a href="/wiki/Geographical_region" class="mw-redirect" title="Geographical region">geographical regions</a> and <a href="/wiki/Plain" title="Plain">terrestrial plains</a> colour-coded</div></figcaption></figure>

</td>
<td style="text-align:center;"><a href="/wiki/Paga_Crocodile_Pond" title="Paga Crocodile Pond">Paga Crocodile Pond</a> location.
</td></tr>
<tr>
<td><a href="/wiki/Cape_Coast" title="Cape Coast">Cape Coast</a>
</td>
<td style="text-align:center;"><a href="/wiki/Cape_Coast_Castle" title="Cape Coast Castle">Cape Coast Castle</a> is a <a href="/wiki/UNESCO_World_Heritage_Site" class="mw-redirect" title="UNESCO World Heritage Site">UNESCO World Heritage Site</a>.
</td></tr>

</td></tr>

</td>
<td style="text-align:center;"><a href="/wiki/Aburi_Botanical_Gardens" title="Aburi Botanical Gardens">Aburi Botanical Gardens</a> location.
</td></tr>
