<td><a href="/wiki/Kumasi" title="Kumasi">Kumasi</a>
</td>
<td style="text-align:center;">Traditional centre of the <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Ashanti" class="mw-redirect" title="Kingdom of Ashanti">Kingdom of Ashanti</a>.
</td></tr>
<tr>
<td><a href="/wiki/Obuasi" title="Obuasi">Obuasi</a>
</td>
<td style="text-align:center;">World's 9th largest <a href="/wiki/Gold_mine" class="mw-redirect" title="Gold mine">gold mine</a> location; and <a href="/wiki/Mining_town" class="mw-redirect" title="Mining town">Mining town</a>.
</td></tr>
<tr>
<td><a href="/wiki/Sekondi-Takoradi" title="Sekondi-Takoradi">Sekondi-Takoradi</a>
</td>
<td style="text-align:center;"><a href="/wiki/Surfing" title="Surfing">Surfing</a> beaches such as <a href="/wiki/Busua_Beach" class="mw-redirect" title="Busua Beach">Busua Beach</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-11_of_the_world&#39;s_most_unusual_surf_spots_80-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-11_of_the_world&#39;s_most_unusual_surf_spots-80">&#91;80&#93;</a></sup> and <a href="/wiki/UNESCO_World_Heritage_Site" class="mw-redirect" title="UNESCO World Heritage Site">UNESCO World Heritage Sites</a>.
</td></tr>
<tr>
<td><a href="/wiki/Tamale,_Ghana" title="Tamale, Ghana">Tamale</a>
</td>
<td style="text-align:center;">Largest settlement in the <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Dagbon" title="Kingdom of Dagbon">Kingdom of Dagbon</a> and gateway to <a href="/wiki/Mole_National_Park" title="Mole National Park">Mole National Park</a>.
</td></tr>
<tr>
<td><a href="/wiki/Yendi,_Ghana" class="mw-redirect" title="Yendi, Ghana">Yendi</a>
</td>
<td style="text-align:center;">Traditional Capital of the <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Dagbon" title="Kingdom of Dagbon">Kingdom of Dagbon</a> and seat of <a href="/wiki/Yaa_Naa" title="Yaa Naa">Yaa Naa</a>.
</td></tr></tbody></table>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Climate_of_Ghana" title="Climate of Ghana">climate of Ghana</a> is <a href="/wiki/Tropical_climate" title="Tropical climate">tropical</a>, and there is <a href="/wiki/Wet_season" title="Wet season">wet season</a> and <a href="/wiki/Dry_season" title="Dry season">dry season</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-UNDP_Climate_81-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-UNDP_Climate-81">&#91;81&#93;</a></sup> Ghana sits at the intersection of 3 hydro-climatic zones.<sup id="cite_ref-:1_82-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:1-82">&#91;82&#93;</a></sup> Changes in rainfall, weather conditions and sea-level rise affect the salinity of coastal waters. This is expected to negatively affect both farming and fisheries.<sup id="cite_ref-83" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-83">&#91;83&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 2015, the government produced a document titled "Ghana's Intended Nationally Determined Contribution."<sup id="cite_ref-:2_84-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:2-84">&#91;84&#93;</a></sup> Following that, Ghana signed the Paris Climate Agreement in 2016.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Politics">Politics</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Government_of_Ghana" title="Government of Ghana">Government of Ghana</a> and <a href="/wiki/Politics_of_Ghana" title="Politics of Ghana">Politics of Ghana</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Ghana_Parliament_House_%E2%80%93_Ghana_Supreme_Court_%E2%80%93_Osu_Castle.JPG" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3b/Ghana_Parliament_House_%E2%80%93_Ghana_Supreme_Court_%E2%80%93_Osu_Castle.JPG/220px-Ghana_Parliament_House_%E2%80%93_Ghana_Supreme_Court_%E2%80%93_Osu_Castle.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="122" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3b/Ghana_Parliament_House_%E2%80%93_Ghana_Supreme_Court_%E2%80%93_Osu_Castle.JPG/330px-Ghana_Parliament_House_%E2%80%93_Ghana_Supreme_Court_%E2%80%93_Osu_Castle.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3b/Ghana_Parliament_House_%E2%80%93_Ghana_Supreme_Court_%E2%80%93_Osu_Castle.JPG/440px-Ghana_Parliament_House_%E2%80%93_Ghana_Supreme_Court_%E2%80%93_Osu_Castle.JPG 2x" data-file-width="833" data-file-height="463" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Parliament_House_of_Ghana" title="Parliament House of Ghana">Parliament House of Ghana</a>, the Supreme Court of Ghana and <a href="/wiki/Judiciary_of_Ghana" title="Judiciary of Ghana">Judiciary of Ghana</a> buildings and <a href="/wiki/Jubilee_House" title="Jubilee House">Jubilee House</a> is the <a href="/wiki/Presidential_palace" title="Presidential palace">presidential palace</a>.</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Presidents_of_Ghana_and_of_the_4th_Republic_of_Ghana.JPG" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2d/Presidents_of_Ghana_and_of_the_4th_Republic_of_Ghana.JPG/220px-Presidents_of_Ghana_and_of_the_4th_Republic_of_Ghana.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2d/Presidents_of_Ghana_and_of_the_4th_Republic_of_Ghana.JPG/330px-Presidents_of_Ghana_and_of_the_4th_Republic_of_Ghana.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2d/Presidents_of_Ghana_and_of_the_4th_Republic_of_Ghana.JPG/440px-Presidents_of_Ghana_and_of_the_4th_Republic_of_Ghana.JPG 2x" data-file-width="675" data-file-height="452" /></a><figcaption>First President of the Republic of Ghana <a href="/wiki/Kwame_Nkrumah" title="Kwame Nkrumah">Nkrumah</a> and the 1st, 2nd, 3rd and 4th presidents of the 4th Republic of Ghana <a href="/wiki/Jerry_Rawlings" title="Jerry Rawlings">Rawlings</a>; <a href="/wiki/John_Kufuor" title="John Kufuor">Kufuor</a>; <a href="/wiki/John_Evans_Atta_Mills" class="mw-redirect" title="John Evans Atta Mills">Mills</a> and <a href="/wiki/John_Dramani_Mahama" class="mw-redirect" title="John Dramani Mahama">Mahama</a>.</figcaption></figure>
<p>Ghana is a <a href="/wiki/Unitary_executive_theory" title="Unitary executive theory">unitary</a> <a href="/wiki/Presidential_system" title="Presidential system">presidential</a> <a href="/wiki/Liberal_democracy" title="Liberal democracy">constitutional democracy</a> with a parliamentary <a href="/wiki/Multi-party_system" title="Multi-party system">multi-party system</a> that is dominated by two parties—the National Democratic Congress (NDC) and the New Patriotic Party (NPP). Ghana alternated between civilian and military governments until January 1993, when the military government gave way to the Fourth Republic of Ghana after <a href="/wiki/1992_Ghanaian_presidential_election" title="1992 Ghanaian presidential election">presidential</a> and <a href="/wiki/1992_Ghanaian_parliamentary_election" title="1992 Ghanaian parliamentary election">parliamentary elections</a> in late 1992. The 1992 <a href="/wiki/Constitution_of_Ghana" title="Constitution of Ghana">constitution of Ghana</a> divides powers among a <a href="/wiki/Commander-in-chief" title="Commander-in-chief">commander-in-chief</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Ghana_Armed_Forces" title="Ghana Armed Forces">Ghana Armed Forces</a> (<a href="/wiki/President_of_Ghana" title="President of Ghana">President of Ghana</a>), parliament (<a href="/wiki/Parliament_of_Ghana" title="Parliament of Ghana">Parliament of Ghana</a>), cabinet (<a href="/wiki/Cabinet_of_Ghana" title="Cabinet of Ghana">Cabinet of Ghana</a>), council of state (<a href="/wiki/Council_of_State_(Ghana)" title="Council of State (Ghana)">Ghanaian Council of State</a>), and an independent judiciary (<a href="/wiki/Judiciary_of_Ghana" title="Judiciary of Ghana">Judiciary of Ghana</a>). The government is elected by <a href="/wiki/Universal_suffrage" title="Universal suffrage">universal suffrage</a> after every four years.<sup id="cite_ref-cs_85-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cs-85">&#91;85&#93;</a></sup>
<a href="/wiki/Nana_Akufo-Addo" title="Nana Akufo-Addo">Nana Akufo-Addo</a> won the presidency in the <a href="/wiki/2016_Ghanaian_general_election" title="2016 Ghanaian general election">general election in 2016</a>, defeating incumbent <a href="/wiki/John_Mahama" title="John Mahama">John Mahama</a>. He also won the <a href="/wiki/2020_Ghanaian_general_election" title="2020 Ghanaian general election">2020 election</a> after the presidential election results were challenged at the Supreme Court by flagbearer of the NDC, John Mahama. Presidents are limited to two four-year terms in office. The president can serve a second term only upon re-election.
The 2012 <a href="/wiki/Fragile_States_Index" title="Fragile States Index">Fragile States Index</a> indicated that Ghana is ranked the 67th-least fragile state in the world and the fifth-least fragile state in Africa. Ghana ranked 112th out of 177 countries on the index.<sup id="cite_ref-86" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-86">&#91;86&#93;</a></sup> Ghana ranked as the 64th-least corrupt and politically corrupt country in the world out of all 174 countries ranked and ranked as the fifth-least corrupt and politically corrupt country in Africa out of 53 countries in the 2012 <a href="/wiki/Corruption_Perceptions_Index" title="Corruption Perceptions Index">Transparency International Corruption Perception Index</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-87" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-87">&#91;87&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-88" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-88">&#91;88&#93;</a></sup> Ghana was ranked 7th in Africa out of 53 countries in the 2012 <a href="/wiki/Ibrahim_Index_of_African_Governance" title="Ibrahim Index of African Governance">Ibrahim Index of African Governance</a>. The Ibrahim Index is a comprehensive measure of African government, based on variables which reflect the success with which governments deliver essential political goods to its citizens.<sup id="cite_ref-89" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-89">&#91;89&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Foreign_relations">Foreign relations</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Foreign_relations_of_Ghana" title="Foreign relations of Ghana">Foreign relations of Ghana</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Kofi_Annan_at_OYW.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ef/Kofi_Annan_at_OYW.jpg/220px-Kofi_Annan_at_OYW.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ef/Kofi_Annan_at_OYW.jpg/330px-Kofi_Annan_at_OYW.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ef/Kofi_Annan_at_OYW.jpg/440px-Kofi_Annan_at_OYW.jpg 2x" data-file-width="800" data-file-height="533" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Kofi_Annan" title="Kofi Annan">Kofi Annan</a>, Ghanaian diplomat and United Nations Secretary-General 1997–2006</figcaption></figure>
<p>Since independence, Ghana has been devoted to ideals of nonalignment and is a founding member of the <a href="/wiki/Non-Aligned_Movement" title="Non-Aligned Movement">Non-Aligned Movement</a>. Ghana favours international and regional political and economic co-operation, and is an active member of the United Nations and the African Union.<sup id="cite_ref-H._E._Mr._Ken_Kanda_90-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-H._E._Mr._Ken_Kanda-90">&#91;90&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Ghana has a strong relationship with the United States. Three recent U.S. presidents—<a href="/wiki/Bill_Clinton" title="Bill Clinton">Bill Clinton</a>, <a href="/wiki/George_W._Bush" title="George W. Bush">George W. Bush</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Barack_Obama" title="Barack Obama">Barack Obama</a> and a Vice President — <a href="/wiki/Kamala_Harris" title="Kamala Harris">Kamala Harris</a> made diplomatic trips to Ghana.<sup id="cite_ref-91" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-91">&#91;91&#93;</a></sup> Many Ghanaian diplomats and politicians hold positions in international organisations, including Ghanaian diplomat and former <a href="/wiki/Secretary-General_of_the_United_Nations" title="Secretary-General of the United Nations">Secretary-General of the United Nations</a> <a href="/wiki/Kofi_Annan" title="Kofi Annan">Kofi Annan</a>, <a href="/wiki/International_Criminal_Court" title="International Criminal Court">International Criminal Court</a> Judge <a href="/wiki/Akua_Kuenyehia" title="Akua Kuenyehia">Akua Kuenyehia</a>, as well as former President <a href="/wiki/Jerry_Rawlings" title="Jerry Rawlings">Jerry John Rawlings</a> and former President <a href="/wiki/John_Kufuor" title="John Kufuor">John Agyekum Kufuor</a>, who both served as diplomats of the United Nations.<sup id="cite_ref-cs_85-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cs-85">&#91;85&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In September 2010, President <a href="/wiki/John_Atta_Mills" title="John Atta Mills">John Atta Mills</a> visited China on an official visit. Mills and China's former President <a href="/wiki/Hu_Jintao" title="Hu Jintao">Hu Jintao</a> marked the 50th anniversary of diplomatic ties between the two nations, at the <a href="/wiki/Great_Hall_of_the_People" title="Great Hall of the People">Great Hall of the People</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Hu_Jintao-John_Atta_Mills_92-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Hu_Jintao-John_Atta_Mills-92">&#91;92&#93;</a></sup> China reciprocated with an official visit in November 2011, by the vice-chairman of the <a href="/wiki/Standing_Committee_of_the_National_People%27s_Congress" title="Standing Committee of the National People&#39;s Congress">Standing Committee of the National People's Congress of China</a>, <a href="/wiki/Zhou_Tienong" title="Zhou Tienong">Zhou Tienong</a> who visited Ghana and met with Ghana's President <a href="/wiki/John_Mahama" title="John Mahama">John Mahama</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Xinhua2011_93-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Xinhua2011-93">&#91;93&#93;</a></sup> Iranian President <a href="/wiki/Mahmoud_Ahmadinejad" title="Mahmoud Ahmadinejad">Mahmoud Ahmadinejad</a> met with Mahama in 2013 to hold discussions on strengthening the <a href="/wiki/Non-Aligned_Movement" title="Non-Aligned Movement">Non-Aligned Movement</a> and also co–chair a bilateral meeting between Ghana and Iran at the Ghanaian <a href="/wiki/Presidential_palace" title="Presidential palace">presidential palace</a> <a href="/wiki/Jubilee_House" title="Jubilee House">Flagstaff House</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Ahmadinejad_94-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ahmadinejad-94">&#91;94&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Ahmadinejad2_95-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ahmadinejad2-95">&#91;95&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-CPP_welcomes_President_Ahmadinejad_visit_to_Ghana_96-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CPP_welcomes_President_Ahmadinejad_visit_to_Ghana-96">&#91;96&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Ghana_welcomed_Iran&#39;s_President_Mahmoud_Ahmadinejad_97-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghana_welcomed_Iran&#39;s_President_Mahmoud_Ahmadinejad-97">&#91;97&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-President_Mahmoud_Ahmadinejad_98-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-President_Mahmoud_Ahmadinejad-98">&#91;98&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Sustainable_Development_Goals_and_Ghana" title="Sustainable Development Goals and Ghana">Sustainable Development Goals (SDG)</a> were integrated into Ghana's development agenda and the budget. According to reports, the SDGs were implemented through a decentralized planning approach. This allows stakeholders' participations such as UN agencies, traditional leaders, civil society organizations, academia, and others.<sup id="cite_ref-99" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-99">&#91;99&#93;</a></sup> The 17 SDGs are a global call to action to end poverty among others, and the UN and its partners in the country are working towards achieving them.<sup id="cite_ref-100" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-100">&#91;100&#93;</a></sup> According to the President <a href="/wiki/Nana_Akufo-Addo" title="Nana Akufo-Addo">Nana Akufo-Addo</a>, Ghana was "the first sub-Saharan African country to achieve the goal of halving poverty, as contained in Goal 1 of the <a href="/wiki/Millennium_Development_Goals" title="Millennium Development Goals">Millennium Development Goals</a>"<sup id="cite_ref-101" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-101">&#91;101&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Military">Military</span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Ghana_Airforce_Reccep_Tayyip_Erdogan_Honor_Guard_at_Flagstaff_House.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/47/Ghana_Airforce_Reccep_Tayyip_Erdogan_Honor_Guard_at_Flagstaff_House.jpg/220px-Ghana_Airforce_Reccep_Tayyip_Erdogan_Honor_Guard_at_Flagstaff_House.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/47/Ghana_Airforce_Reccep_Tayyip_Erdogan_Honor_Guard_at_Flagstaff_House.jpg/330px-Ghana_Airforce_Reccep_Tayyip_Erdogan_Honor_Guard_at_Flagstaff_House.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/47/Ghana_Airforce_Reccep_Tayyip_Erdogan_Honor_Guard_at_Flagstaff_House.jpg/440px-Ghana_Airforce_Reccep_Tayyip_Erdogan_Honor_Guard_at_Flagstaff_House.jpg 2x" data-file-width="5472" data-file-height="3648" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Turkish_President" class="mw-redirect" title="Turkish President">Turkish President</a> <a href="/wiki/Recep_Tayyip_Erdo%C4%9Fan" title="Recep Tayyip Erdoğan">Recep Tayyip Erdoğan</a> inspects <a href="/wiki/Guard_of_honour" title="Guard of honour">Honour Guards</a> mounted by the <a href="/wiki/Ghana_Air_Force" title="Ghana Air Force">Ghana Air Force</a> at the <a href="/wiki/Jubilee_House" title="Jubilee House">Jubilee House</a>, the Presidential Palace of Ghana in <a href="/wiki/Greater_Accra_Region" title="Greater Accra Region">Greater Accra</a> on 1 March 2016.</figcaption></figure>
<p>In 1957, the <a href="/wiki/Ghana_Armed_Forces" title="Ghana Armed Forces">Ghana Armed Forces</a> (GAF) consisted of its headquarters, support services, three battalions of infantry and a reconnaissance squadron with armoured vehicles.<sup id="cite_ref-Canada&#39;s_Military_Assistance_102-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Canada&#39;s_Military_Assistance-102">&#91;102&#93;</a></sup> President Nkrumah aimed at rapidly expanding the GAF to support the <a href="/wiki/United_States_of_Africa" title="United States of Africa">United States of Africa</a> ambitions. Thus, in 1961, 4th and 5th Battalions were established, and in 1964 6th Battalion was established, from a <a href="/wiki/Paratrooper" title="Paratrooper">parachute</a> <a href="/wiki/Airborne_forces" title="Airborne forces">airborne unit</a> originally raised in 1963.<sup id="cite_ref-103" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-103">&#91;103&#93;</a></sup> Today, Ghana is a <a href="/wiki/Regional_power" title="Regional power">regional power</a> and <a href="/wiki/Regional_hegemony" title="Regional hegemony">regional hegemon</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-South_America_and_West_Africa_22-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-South_America_and_West_Africa-22">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup> In his book <i><a href="/wiki/Shake_Hands_with_the_Devil_(book)" title="Shake Hands with the Devil (book)">Shake Hands with the Devil</a></i>, <a href="/wiki/Canadian_Armed_Forces" title="Canadian Armed Forces">Canadian Forces</a> commander <a href="/wiki/Rom%C3%A9o_Dallaire" title="Roméo Dallaire">Roméo Dallaire</a> highly rated the GAF soldiers and military personnel.<sup id="cite_ref-Canada&#39;s_Military_Assistance_102-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Canada&#39;s_Military_Assistance-102">&#91;102&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Military_operation" title="Military operation">military operations</a> and <a href="/wiki/Military_doctrine" title="Military doctrine">military doctrine</a> of the GAF are conceptualised in the constitution, Ghana's Law on Armed Force Military Strategy, and <a href="/wiki/Kofi_Annan_International_Peacekeeping_Training_Centre" title="Kofi Annan International Peacekeeping Training Centre">Kofi Annan International Peacekeeping Training Centre</a> agreements to which GAF is attestator.<sup id="cite_ref-Defence_104-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Defence-104">&#91;104&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Ghana&#39;s_Regional_Security_Policy_105-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghana&#39;s_Regional_Security_Policy-105">&#91;105&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-KAIPTC_106-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-KAIPTC-106">&#91;106&#93;</a></sup> GAF military operations are executed under the auspices and imperium of the <a href="/wiki/Ministry_of_Defence_(Ghana)" title="Ministry of Defence (Ghana)">Ministry of Defence</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Defence_104-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Defence-104">&#91;104&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Vision_&amp;_Mission_107-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Vision_&amp;_Mission-107">&#91;107&#93;</a></sup> Although Ghana is relatively peaceful and is often considered being one of the least violent countries in the region, Ghana has experienced political violence in the past and 2017 has thus far seen an upward trend in incidents motivated by political grievances.<sup id="cite_ref-108" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-108">&#91;108&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Law_enforcement">Law enforcement</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/Law_enforcement_in_Ghana" title="Law enforcement in Ghana">Law enforcement in Ghana</a> and <a href="/wiki/Crime_in_Ghana" title="Crime in Ghana">Crime in Ghana</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Ghana_Police_Service_Motorbikes.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b5/Ghana_Police_Service_Motorbikes.jpg/220px-Ghana_Police_Service_Motorbikes.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="114" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b5/Ghana_Police_Service_Motorbikes.jpg/330px-Ghana_Police_Service_Motorbikes.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/b/b5/Ghana_Police_Service_Motorbikes.jpg 2x" data-file-width="413" data-file-height="214" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Militarized_police" class="mw-redirect" title="Militarized police">Militarized police</a> Unit of the <a href="/wiki/Ghana_Police_Service" title="Ghana Police Service">Ghana Police Service</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Ghana_Police_Service" title="Ghana Police Service">Ghana Police Service</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Criminal_Investigation_Department" title="Criminal Investigation Department">Criminal Investigation Department</a> are the main law enforcement agencies, responsible for the detection of crime, maintenance of law and order and the maintenance of internal peace and security.<sup id="cite_ref-MINT_109-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-MINT-109">&#91;109&#93;</a></sup> The Ghana Police Service has eleven specialised police units, including a <a href="/wiki/Militarized_police" class="mw-redirect" title="Militarized police">Militarized police</a> <a href="/wiki/Rapid_deployment_force" class="mw-redirect" title="Rapid deployment force">Rapid deployment force</a> and <a href="/wiki/Ghana_Police_Service#Marine_Police_Unit" title="Ghana Police Service">Marine Police Unit</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-UNIT_110-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-UNIT-110">&#91;110&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-POLICE_111-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-POLICE-111">&#91;111&#93;</a></sup> The Ghana Police Service operates in 12 divisions: ten covering the regions of Ghana, one assigned specifically to the seaport and industrial hub of <a href="/wiki/Tema" title="Tema">Tema</a>, and the twelfth being the Railways, Ports and Harbours Division.<sup id="cite_ref-POLICE_111-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-POLICE-111">&#91;111&#93;</a></sup> The Ghana Police Service's Marine Police Unit and Division handles issues that arise from the country's offshore <a href="/wiki/Oil_and_gas_industry" class="mw-redirect" title="Oil and gas industry">oil and gas industry</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-POLICE_111-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-POLICE-111">&#91;111&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>
The <a href="/wiki/Ghana_Prisons_Service" title="Ghana Prisons Service">Ghana Prisons Service</a> and the sub-division <a href="/wiki/Borstal_Institute_for_Juveniles" title="Borstal Institute for Juveniles">Borstal Institute for Juveniles</a> administers incarceration.<sup id="cite_ref-Ghana_Prisons_Service_General_Information_112-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghana_Prisons_Service_General_Information-112">&#91;112&#93;</a></sup> Ghana retains and exercises the death penalty for treason, corruption, robbery, piracy, drug trafficking, rape, and homicide.<sup id="cite_ref-Ghana_–_Death_Penalty_113-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghana_–_Death_Penalty-113">&#91;113&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Criminal_Code_and_Courts_114-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Criminal_Code_and_Courts-114">&#91;114&#93;</a></sup> The new sustainable development goals adopted by the United Nations call for the international community to come together to promote the rule of law; support equal access to justice for all; reduce corruption; and develop effective, accountable, and transparent institutions at all levels.<sup id="cite_ref-DIPNote:_Promoting_Peace,_Justice,_and_Strong_Institutions_in_the_Great_Lakes_115-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-DIPNote:_Promoting_Peace,_Justice,_and_Strong_Institutions_in_the_Great_Lakes-115">&#91;115&#93;</a></sup></p><figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Drugtrade.png" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2c/Drugtrade.png/220px-Drugtrade.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2c/Drugtrade.png/330px-Drugtrade.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2c/Drugtrade.png/440px-Drugtrade.png 2x" data-file-width="1024" data-file-height="768" /></a><figcaption>Ghana is among the sovereign states of West Africa used by <a href="/wiki/Drug_cartel" title="Drug cartel">drug cartels</a> and drug traffickers (shown in orange).</figcaption></figure>
<p>Ghana is used as a key narcotics industry transshipment point by traffickers, usually from South America as well as some from other African nations.<sup id="cite_ref-Ghana_hit_by_illegal_drug_trade_116-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghana_hit_by_illegal_drug_trade-116">&#91;116&#93;</a></sup> In 2013, the UN chief of the Office on Drugs and Crime stated that "West Africa is completely weak in terms of border control and the big drug cartels from <a href="/wiki/Colombia" title="Colombia">Colombia</a> and <a href="/wiki/Latin_America" title="Latin America">Latin America</a> have chosen Africa as a way to reach Europe."<sup id="cite_ref-DW_Made_for_minds:_Illegal_drug_use_on_the_rise_in_Africa_117-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-DW_Made_for_minds:_Illegal_drug_use_on_the_rise_in_Africa-117">&#91;117&#93;</a></sup> There is not a wide or popular knowledge about the narcotics industry and intercepted narcotics within Ghana, since it is an <a href="/wiki/Black_market" title="Black market">underground economy</a>. The social context within which narcotic trafficking, storage, transportation, and repacking systems exist in Ghana and the state's location along the Gulf of Guinea makes Ghana an attractive country for the narcotics business.<sup id="cite_ref-Ghana_hit_by_illegal_drug_trade_116-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghana_hit_by_illegal_drug_trade-116">&#91;116&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Ghana_could_be_taken_over_by_drug_barons_if_118-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghana_could_be_taken_over_by_drug_barons_if-118">&#91;118&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Narcotics_Control_Board_(Ghana)" title="Narcotics Control Board (Ghana)">Narcotics Control Board</a> has impounded container ships at the Sekondi Naval Base in the <a href="/wiki/Takoradi_Harbour" title="Takoradi Harbour">Takoradi Harbour</a>. These ships were carrying thousands of kilograms of cocaine, with a street value running into billions of <a href="/wiki/Ghanaian_cedi" title="Ghanaian cedi">Ghana cedis</a>. However, drug seizures saw a decline in 2011.<sup id="cite_ref-Ghana_hit_by_illegal_drug_trade_116-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghana_hit_by_illegal_drug_trade-116">&#91;116&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Ghana_could_be_taken_over_by_drug_barons_if_118-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghana_could_be_taken_over_by_drug_barons_if-118">&#91;118&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Drug_cartel" title="Drug cartel">Drug cartels</a> are using new methods in narcotics production and narcotics exportation, to avoid Ghanaian security agencies.<sup id="cite_ref-Ghana_hit_by_illegal_drug_trade_116-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghana_hit_by_illegal_drug_trade-116">&#91;116&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Ghana_could_be_taken_over_by_drug_barons_if_118-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghana_could_be_taken_over_by_drug_barons_if-118">&#91;118&#93;</a></sup> Underdeveloped institutions, porous open borders, and the existence of established smuggling organisations contribute to Ghana's position in the narcotics industry.<sup id="cite_ref-Ghana_hit_by_illegal_drug_trade_116-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghana_hit_by_illegal_drug_trade-116">&#91;116&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Ghana_could_be_taken_over_by_drug_barons_if_118-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghana_could_be_taken_over_by_drug_barons_if-118">&#91;118&#93;</a></sup> President Mills initiated ongoing efforts to reduce the role of airports in Ghana's drug trade.<sup id="cite_ref-Ghana_hit_by_illegal_drug_trade_116-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghana_hit_by_illegal_drug_trade-116">&#91;116&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Human_rights">Human rights</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Human_rights_in_Ghana" title="Human rights in Ghana">Human rights in Ghana</a> and <a href="/wiki/LGBT_rights_in_Ghana" title="LGBT rights in Ghana">LGBT rights in Ghana</a></div>
<p><a href="/wiki/Homosexuality" title="Homosexuality">Homosexual acts</a> are prohibited by law in Ghana.<sup id="cite_ref-119" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-119">&#91;119&#93;</a></sup> According to a 2013 survey by the <a href="/wiki/Pew_Research_Center" title="Pew Research Center">Pew Research Center</a>, 96% of Ghanaians believe that homosexuality should not be accepted by society.<sup id="cite_ref-pewglobal.org_120-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-pewglobal.org-120">&#91;120&#93;</a></sup> Sometimes elderly <a href="/wiki/Women_in_Ghana" title="Women in Ghana">women in Ghana</a> are accused of <a href="/wiki/Witchcraft" title="Witchcraft">witchcraft</a>, particularly in rural Ghana. Issues of witchcraft mainly remain as speculations based on superstitions within families. In some parts of northern Ghana, there exist what are called <a href="/wiki/Witch_camp" title="Witch camp">witch camps</a>. This is said to house a total of around 1,000 people accused of witchcraft.<sup id="cite_ref-Camps_121-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Camps-121">&#91;121&#93;</a></sup> The Ghanaian government has announced that it intends to close the camps.<sup id="cite_ref-Camps_121-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Camps-121">&#91;121&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Economy">Economy</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Economy_of_Ghana" title="Economy of Ghana">Economy of Ghana</a>, <a href="/wiki/New_media_in_Ghana" title="New media in Ghana">New media in Ghana</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Manufacturing_in_Ghana#Automobile_manufacturing" title="Manufacturing in Ghana">Automobile manufacturing in Ghana</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:GDP_per_capita_development_development_of_Ghana.svg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5d/GDP_per_capita_development_development_of_Ghana.svg/220px-GDP_per_capita_development_development_of_Ghana.svg.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="155" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5d/GDP_per_capita_development_development_of_Ghana.svg/330px-GDP_per_capita_development_development_of_Ghana.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5d/GDP_per_capita_development_development_of_Ghana.svg/440px-GDP_per_capita_development_development_of_Ghana.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="850" data-file-height="600" /></a><figcaption>Change in per capita GDP, 1870–2018. Figures are inflation-adjusted to 2011 International dollars</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Ghana_Product_Exports_(2019).svg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a9/Ghana_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/290px-Ghana_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png" decoding="async" width="290" height="189" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a9/Ghana_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/435px-Ghana_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a9/Ghana_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/580px-Ghana_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="1100" data-file-height="715" /></a><figcaption>A proportional representation of exports, 2019</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Ghana_Export_Trends.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e1/Ghana_Export_Trends.jpg/220px-Ghana_Export_Trends.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e1/Ghana_Export_Trends.jpg/330px-Ghana_Export_Trends.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e1/Ghana_Export_Trends.jpg/440px-Ghana_Export_Trends.jpg 2x" data-file-width="960" data-file-height="720" /></a><figcaption>Ghana Petroleum and <a href="/wiki/Commodity" title="Commodity">commodities</a>; exports in <a href="/wiki/Percentage" title="Percentage">percentage</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>Ghana possesses <a href="/wiki/Industrial_mineral" title="Industrial mineral">industrial minerals</a>, <a href="/wiki/Hydrocarbon" title="Hydrocarbon">hydrocarbons</a> and <a href="/wiki/Precious_metal" title="Precious metal">precious metals</a>. It is an emerging designated <a href="/wiki/Digital_economy" title="Digital economy">digital economy</a> with <a href="/wiki/Mixed_economy" title="Mixed economy">mixed economy</a> hybridisation and an <a href="/wiki/Emerging_market" title="Emerging market">emerging market</a>. It has an economic plan target known as the "Ghana Vision 2020". This plan envisions Ghana as the first African country to become a <a href="/wiki/Developed_country" title="Developed country">developed country</a> between 2020 and 2029 and a <a href="/wiki/Newly_industrialized_country" title="Newly industrialized country">newly industrialised country</a> between 2030 and 2039.<sup id="cite_ref-122" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-122">&#91;122&#93;</a></sup> This excludes fellow <a href="/wiki/Group_of_24" title="Group of 24">Group of 24</a> member and <a href="/wiki/Sub-Saharan_Africa" title="Sub-Saharan Africa">Sub-Saharan African</a> country South Africa, which is a newly industrialised country.<sup id="cite_ref-123" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-123">&#91;123&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Ghana's economy has ties to the <a href="/wiki/Renminbi" title="Renminbi">Chinese yuan renminbi</a> along with Ghana's vast gold reserves. In 2013, the <a href="/wiki/Bank_of_Ghana" title="Bank of Ghana">Bank of Ghana</a> began circulating the renminbi throughout Ghanaian state-owned banks and to the Ghana public as <a href="/wiki/Hard_currency" title="Hard currency">hard currency</a> along with the national <a href="/wiki/Ghanaian_cedi" title="Ghanaian cedi">Ghanaian cedi</a> for second national trade currency.<sup id="cite_ref-BoG_introduce_Chinese_Yuan_onto_the_FX_market_124-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-BoG_introduce_Chinese_Yuan_onto_the_FX_market-124">&#91;124&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Between 2012 and 2013, 38% of rural dwellers were experiencing poverty whereas only 11% of urban dwellers were.<sup id="cite_ref-125" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-125">&#91;125&#93;</a></sup> Urban areas hold greater opportunity for employment, particularly in informal trade, while nearly all (94 percent) of "rural poor households" participate in the agricultural sector.<sup id="cite_ref-126" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-126">&#91;126&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Volta_River_Authority" title="Volta River Authority">Volta River Authority</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Ghana_National_Petroleum_Corporation" title="Ghana National Petroleum Corporation">Ghana National Petroleum Corporation</a>, both state-owned, are the two major electricity producers.<sup id="cite_ref-Ghana_–_Gross_Domestic_Product_127-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghana_–_Gross_Domestic_Product-127">&#91;127&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Akosombo_Dam" title="Akosombo Dam">Akosombo Dam</a>, built on the Volta River in 1965, along with the <a href="/wiki/Bui_Dam" title="Bui Dam">Bui Dam</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Kpong_Dam" title="Kpong Dam">Kpong Dam</a> and several other hydroelectric dams, provide <a href="/wiki/Hydropower" title="Hydropower">hydropower</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-128" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-128">&#91;128&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-New_fuel_for_faster_development_129-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-New_fuel_for_faster_development-129">&#91;129&#93;</a></sup> In addition, the government sought to <a href="/wiki/Nuclear_power_in_Ghana" title="Nuclear power in Ghana">build the second nuclear power plant in Africa</a>.
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Ghana_Stock_Exchange" title="Ghana Stock Exchange">Ghana Stock Exchange</a> is the 5th largest on continental Africa and 3rd largest in sub-saharan Africa with a <a href="/wiki/Market_capitalization" title="Market capitalization">market capitalisation</a> of <a href="/wiki/Ghana_Cedi" class="mw-redirect" title="Ghana Cedi">GH¢</a> 57.2&#160;billion or <a href="/wiki/Renminbi" title="Renminbi">CN¥</a>180.4&#160;billion in 2012 with the South Africa <a href="/wiki/JSE_Limited" title="JSE Limited">JSE Limited</a> as first.<sup id="cite_ref-130" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-130">&#91;130&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Ghana_Stock_Exchange" title="Ghana Stock Exchange">Ghana Stock Exchange</a> was the 2nd best performing <a href="/wiki/Stock_exchange" title="Stock exchange">stock exchange</a> in sub-saharan Africa in 2013.<sup id="cite_ref-131" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-131">&#91;131&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Ghana produces high-quality <a href="/wiki/Cocoa_bean" title="Cocoa bean">cocoa</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Ghana_Entering_A_Sweet,_Golden_Era_132-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghana_Entering_A_Sweet,_Golden_Era-132">&#91;132&#93;</a></sup> It is the 2nd largest producer of cocoa globally.<sup id="cite_ref-133" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-133">&#91;133&#93;</a></sup> Ghana is classified as a middle income country.<sup id="cite_ref-IMF_6-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-IMF-6">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-134" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-134">&#91;134&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Tertiary_sector_of_the_economy" title="Tertiary sector of the economy">Services</a> account for 50% of GDP, followed by <a href="/wiki/Secondary_sector_of_the_economy" title="Secondary sector of the economy">manufacturing</a> (24.1%), <a href="/wiki/Primary_sector_of_the_economy" title="Primary sector of the economy">extractive industries</a> (5%), and taxes (20.9%).<sup id="cite_ref-Ghana_–_Gross_Domestic_Product_127-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghana_–_Gross_Domestic_Product-127">&#91;127&#93;</a></sup> Ghana has an increasing primary manufacturing economy and export of digital technology goods along with assembling and exporting automobiles and ships, diverse resource rich exportation of <a href="/wiki/Industrial_mineral" title="Industrial mineral">industrial minerals</a>, agricultural products primarily cocoa, petroleum and natural gas,<sup id="cite_ref-Ghana&#39;s_Jubilee_oil_field_nears_output_plateau_-operator_135-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghana&#39;s_Jubilee_oil_field_nears_output_plateau_-operator-135">&#91;135&#93;</a></sup> and <a href="/wiki/Private_industry" class="mw-redirect" title="Private industry">industries</a> such as <a href="/wiki/Information_and_communications_technology" title="Information and communications technology">information and communications technology</a> primarily via Ghana's state digital technology corporation <a href="/wiki/Rlg_Communications" title="Rlg Communications">Rlg Communications</a> which manufactures <a href="/wiki/Tablet_computer" title="Tablet computer">tablet computers</a> with <a href="/wiki/Smartphone" title="Smartphone">smartphones</a> and various <a href="/wiki/Consumer_electronics" title="Consumer electronics">consumer electronics</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Ghana_–_Gross_Domestic_Product_127-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghana_–_Gross_Domestic_Product-127">&#91;127&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-The_Top_5_Countries_for_ICT_in_Africa_136-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-The_Top_5_Countries_for_ICT_in_Africa-136">&#91;136&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Urban_car" class="mw-redirect" title="Urban car">Urban</a> <a href="/wiki/Electric_car" title="Electric car">electric cars</a> have been manufactured in Ghana since 2014.<sup id="cite_ref-137" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-137">&#91;137&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-138" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-138">&#91;138&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>It announced plans to issue government debt by way of social and green bonds in Autumn 2021 making it the first African country to do so.<sup id="cite_ref-139" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-139">&#91;139&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-140" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-140">&#91;140&#93;</a></sup> The country, which is planning to borrow up to $5 billion in international markets this year, would use the proceeds from these sustainable bonds to refinance debt used for social and environmental projects and pay for educational or health. Only a few other nations have sold them so far, including <a href="/wiki/Chile" title="Chile">Chile</a> and <a href="/wiki/Ecuador" title="Ecuador">Ecuador</a>. The country will use the proceeds to forge ahead with a free secondary-school initiative started in 2017 among other programs, despite having recorded its lowest economic growth rate in 37 years in 2020.<sup id="cite_ref-141" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-141">&#91;141&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Jubilee_Oil_Field_of_the_Ghana_National_Petroleum_Corporation_(GNPC)_and_National_Petroleum_Authority.png" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0d/Jubilee_Oil_Field_of_the_Ghana_National_Petroleum_Corporation_%28GNPC%29_and_National_Petroleum_Authority.png/220px-Jubilee_Oil_Field_of_the_Ghana_National_Petroleum_Corporation_%28GNPC%29_and_National_Petroleum_Authority.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="122" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0d/Jubilee_Oil_Field_of_the_Ghana_National_Petroleum_Corporation_%28GNPC%29_and_National_Petroleum_Authority.png/330px-Jubilee_Oil_Field_of_the_Ghana_National_Petroleum_Corporation_%28GNPC%29_and_National_Petroleum_Authority.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/0/0d/Jubilee_Oil_Field_of_the_Ghana_National_Petroleum_Corporation_%28GNPC%29_and_National_Petroleum_Authority.png 2x" data-file-width="379" data-file-height="210" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Jubilee_oil_field" class="mw-redirect" title="Jubilee oil field">Jubilee oil field</a> of the Ghana National Petroleum Corporation (GNPC) and <a href="/wiki/National_Petroleum_Authority" title="National Petroleum Authority">National Petroleum Authority</a> located off the coast of the <a href="/wiki/Western_Region_(Ghana)" title="Western Region (Ghana)">Western Region</a> in the <a href="/wiki/South_Atlantic_Ocean" class="mw-redirect" title="South Atlantic Ocean">South Atlantic Ocean</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>It produces and exports <a href="/wiki/Hydrocarbon" title="Hydrocarbon">hydrocarbons</a> such as <a href="/wiki/Sweet_crude_oil" title="Sweet crude oil">sweet crude oil</a> and <a href="/wiki/Natural_gas" title="Natural gas">natural gas</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Five_Countries_to_Watch_142-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Five_Countries_to_Watch-142">&#91;142&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-143" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-143">&#91;143&#93;</a></sup> The 100%-state-owned <a href="/wiki/Filling_station" title="Filling station">filling station</a> company, <a href="/wiki/Ghana_Oil_Company" title="Ghana Oil Company">Ghana Oil Company</a>, is the number 1 petroleum and gas filling station, and the 100%-state-owned state oil company <a href="/wiki/Ghana_National_Petroleum_Corporation" title="Ghana National Petroleum Corporation">Ghana National Petroleum Corporation</a> oversees <a href="/wiki/Hydrocarbon_exploration" title="Hydrocarbon exploration">hydrocarbon exploration</a> and production of petroleum and natural gas reserves. Ghana aims to further increase the output of oil to 2.2&#160;million barrels (350,000&#160;m<sup>3</sup>) per day and gas to 34,000,000 cubic metres (1.2<span style="margin-left:0.2em">×<span style="margin-left:0.1em">10</span></span><s style="display:none">^</s><sup>9</sup>&#160;cu&#160;ft) per day.<sup id="cite_ref-cs2_144-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cs2-144">&#91;144&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Jubilee_Oil_Field" title="Jubilee Oil Field">Jubilee Oil Field</a>, which contains up to 3 billion barrels (480,000,000&#160;m<sup>3</sup>) of sweet crude oil, was discovered in 2007.<sup id="cite_ref-145" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-145">&#91;145&#93;</a></sup> Ghana is believed to have up to 5 billion barrels (790,000,000&#160;m<sup>3</sup>) to 7 billion barrels (1.1<span style="margin:0 .15em 0 .25em">×</span>10<sup>9</sup>&#160;m<sup>3</sup>) of petroleum in reserves,<sup id="cite_ref-146" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-146">&#91;146&#93;</a></sup> which is the fifth-largest in Africa and the 21st-to-25th-<a href="/wiki/List_of_countries_by_proven_oil_reserves" title="List of countries by proven oil reserves">largest proven reserves</a> in the world. It also has up to 1.7<span style="margin:0 .15em 0 .25em">×</span>10<sup>11</sup> cubic metres (6<span style="margin-left:0.2em">×<span style="margin-left:0.1em">10</span></span><s style="display:none">^</s><sup>12</sup>&#160;cu&#160;ft) of natural gas in reserves.<sup id="cite_ref-Atuabo_gas_project_to_propel_more_growth_147-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Atuabo_gas_project_to_propel_more_growth-147">&#91;147&#93;</a></sup> The government has drawn up plans to <a href="/wiki/Nationalization" title="Nationalization">nationalise</a> petroleum and natural gas reserves to increase government revenue.<sup id="cite_ref-148" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-148">&#91;148&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>As of 2019, Ghana was the 7th largest producer of gold in the world, producing ~140 <a href="/wiki/Tonne" title="Tonne">tonnes</a> that year.<sup id="cite_ref-CEIC_149-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CEIC-149">&#91;149&#93;</a></sup> This record saw Ghana surpass South Africa in output for the first time, making Ghana the largest gold producer in Africa.<sup id="cite_ref-150" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-150">&#91;150&#93;</a></sup> In addition to gold, Ghana exports <a href="/wiki/Silver" title="Silver">silver</a>, timber, <a href="/wiki/Diamond" title="Diamond">diamonds</a>, <a href="/wiki/Bauxite" title="Bauxite">bauxite</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Manganese" title="Manganese">manganese</a>, and has other mineral deposits.<sup id="cite_ref-151" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-151">&#91;151&#93;</a></sup> Ghana ranks 9th in the world in diamond export and reserve size.<sup id="cite_ref-152" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-152">&#91;152&#93;</a></sup> The government has drawn up plans to <a href="/wiki/Nationalization" title="Nationalization">nationalize</a> mining industry to increase government revenue.<sup id="cite_ref-153" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-153">&#91;153&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-154" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-154">&#91;154&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>"Shortages" of electricity in 2015 &amp; 2016 led to <a href="/wiki/Dumsor" title="Dumsor">dumsor</a> ("persistent, irregular and unpredictable" electric power outages),<sup id="cite_ref-germany_155-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-germany-155">&#91;155&#93;</a></sup> increasing the interest in renewables.<sup id="cite_ref-graphic1_156-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-graphic1-156">&#91;156&#93;</a></sup> As of 2019, there is a surplus of electricity.<sup id="cite_ref-157" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-157">&#91;157&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Judiciary_of_Ghana" title="Judiciary of Ghana">judicial system of Ghana</a> deals with corruption, economic malpractice and lack of economic transparency.<sup id="cite_ref-Ghana_Economy_–_heritage_158-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghana_Economy_–_heritage-158">&#91;158&#93;</a></sup> According to Transparency International's Corruption Perception Index of 2018, out of 180 countries, Ghana was ranked 78th, with a score of 41 on a scale where a 0–9 score means highly corrupt, and a 90–100 score means very clean. This was based on perceived levels of public sector corruption.<sup id="cite_ref-159" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-159">&#91;159&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Science_and_technology">Science and technology</span></h3>
<p>It launched a cellular mobile network (1992). It was connected to the internet and introduced ADSL broadband services.<sup id="cite_ref-Ghanaweb_160-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghanaweb-160">&#91;160&#93;</a></sup> It was ranked 112nd in the <a href="/wiki/Global_Innovation_Index" title="Global Innovation Index">Global Innovation Index</a> in 2021, down from 106th in 2019.<sup id="cite_ref-161" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-161">&#91;161&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-162" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-162">&#91;162&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-163" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-163">&#91;163&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-164" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-164">&#91;164&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Ghana_Space_Science_and_Technology_Centre" title="Ghana Space Science and Technology Centre">Ghana Space Science and Technology Centre</a> (GSSTC) and Ghana Space Agency (GhsA) oversee <a href="/wiki/Space_exploration" title="Space exploration">space exploration</a> and space programmes. GSSTC and GhsA worked to have a <a href="/wiki/National_security" title="National security">national security</a> <a href="/wiki/Earth_observation_satellite" title="Earth observation satellite">observational satellite</a> launched into orbit in 2015.<sup id="cite_ref-BBCSpace_165-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-BBCSpace-165">&#91;165&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-166" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-166">&#91;166&#93;</a></sup> Ghana's annual space exploration expenditure has been 1% of its GDP, to support research in science and technology. In 2012, Ghana was elected to chair the <a href="/wiki/Commission_on_Science_and_Technology_for_Sustainable_Development_in_the_South" title="Commission on Science and Technology for Sustainable Development in the South">Commission on Science and Technology for Sustainable Development in the South</a> (Comsats); Ghana has a joint effort in space exploration with the <a href="/wiki/South_African_National_Space_Agency" title="South African National Space Agency">South African National Space Agency</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-BBCSpace_165-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-BBCSpace-165">&#91;165&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Tourism">Tourism</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Tourism_in_Ghana" title="Tourism in Ghana">Tourism in Ghana</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Surfers_Surfing_at_Busua_Beach_in_Western_region,_Ghana.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6b/Surfers_Surfing_at_Busua_Beach_in_Western_region%2C_Ghana.jpg/220px-Surfers_Surfing_at_Busua_Beach_in_Western_region%2C_Ghana.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6b/Surfers_Surfing_at_Busua_Beach_in_Western_region%2C_Ghana.jpg/330px-Surfers_Surfing_at_Busua_Beach_in_Western_region%2C_Ghana.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6b/Surfers_Surfing_at_Busua_Beach_in_Western_region%2C_Ghana.jpg/440px-Surfers_Surfing_at_Busua_Beach_in_Western_region%2C_Ghana.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1024" data-file-height="768" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Surfer" class="mw-redirect" title="Surfer">Surfers</a> <a href="/wiki/Surfing" title="Surfing">surfing</a> and <a href="/wiki/Big_wave_surfing" title="Big wave surfing">big wave surfing</a> at <a href="/wiki/Busua_Beach" class="mw-redirect" title="Busua Beach">Busua Beach</a> in <a href="/wiki/Western_Region_(Ghana)" title="Western Region (Ghana)">Western region</a><sup id="cite_ref-11_of_the_world&#39;s_most_unusual_surf_spots_80-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-11_of_the_world&#39;s_most_unusual_surf_spots-80">&#91;80&#93;</a></sup></figcaption></figure>
<p>In 2011, 1,087,000 tourists visited Ghana.<sup id="cite_ref-WAO_167-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-WAO-167">&#91;167&#93;</a></sup> Tourist arrivals include South Americans, Asians, Europeans, and North Americans.<sup id="cite_ref-Trade_Expo_International_Ghana_168-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Trade_Expo_International_Ghana-168">&#91;168&#93;</a></sup> The attractions and tourist destinations include waterfalls such as <a href="/wiki/Kintampo_waterfalls" title="Kintampo waterfalls">Kintampo waterfalls</a> and the largest waterfall in west Africa, <a href="/wiki/Wli_waterfalls" title="Wli waterfalls">Wli waterfalls</a>, the coastal palm-lined sandy beaches, caves, mountains, rivers, and reservoirs and lakes such as <a href="/wiki/Lake_Bosumtwi" title="Lake Bosumtwi">Lake Bosumtwi</a> and the largest human-made lake in the world by surface area, <a href="/wiki/Lake_Volta" title="Lake Volta">Lake Volta</a>, dozens of <a href="/wiki/List_of_castles_in_Ghana" title="List of castles in Ghana">forts and castles</a>, <a href="/wiki/World_Heritage_Site" title="World Heritage Site">World Heritage Sites</a>, nature reserves and national parks.<sup id="cite_ref-Trade_Expo_International_Ghana_168-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Trade_Expo_International_Ghana-168">&#91;168&#93;</a></sup> Some castles are <a href="/wiki/Cape_Coast_Castle_Museum" title="Cape Coast Castle Museum">Cape Coast Castle</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Elmina_Castle" title="Elmina Castle">Elmina Castle</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-169" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-169">&#91;169&#93;</a></sup> Castles mark where blood was shed in the slave trade and preserve and promote the African heritage stolen and destroyed through the slave trade.<sup id="cite_ref-Centre_170-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Centre-170">&#91;170&#93;</a></sup> As a result of this, the <a href="/wiki/World_Heritage_Site" title="World Heritage Site">World Heritage Convention</a> of UNESCO named Ghana's castles and forts as World Heritage Monuments.<sup id="cite_ref-Centre_170-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Centre-170">&#91;170&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/World_Economic_Forum" title="World Economic Forum">World Economic Forum</a> statistics in 2010 showed that out of the world's favourite tourist destinations, Ghana was ranked 108th out of 139 countries.<sup id="cite_ref-RANK_171-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-RANK-171">&#91;171&#93;</a></sup> The country had moved 2 places up from the 2009 rankings. In 2011, <i><a href="/wiki/Forbes" title="Forbes">Forbes</a></i> magazine published that Ghana was ranked the eleventh most friendly country in the world. The assertion was based on a survey in 2010 of a cross-section of travellers. Of all the African countries that were included in the survey, Ghana ranked highest.<sup id="cite_ref-RANK_171-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-RANK-171">&#91;171&#93;</a></sup> Tourism is the fourth highest earner of foreign exchange for the country.<sup id="cite_ref-RANK_171-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-RANK-171">&#91;171&#93;</a></sup> In 2017, Ghana ranked as the <a href="/wiki/Global_Peace_Index" title="Global Peace Index">43rd–most peaceful country</a> in the world.<sup id="cite_ref-About_the_Global_Peace_Index_172-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-About_the_Global_Peace_Index-172">&#91;172&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Up and down the coastline, surfing spots have been identified and cultivated by locals and internationals. Surfers have made trips to the country to sample the waves. Surfers carried their boards amid <a href="/wiki/Traditional_fishing_boat" title="Traditional fishing boat">traditional fishing vessels</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-173" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-173">&#91;173&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>According to Destination Pride<sup id="cite_ref-174" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-174">&#91;174&#93;</a></sup>–a data-driven search platform used to visualize the world's LGBTQ+ laws, rights and social sentiment–Ghana's Pride score is 22 (out of 100).<sup id="cite_ref-175" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-175">&#91;175&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Demographics">Demographics</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Demographics_of_Ghana" title="Demographics of Ghana">Demographics of Ghana</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/Ghanaian_people" class="mw-redirect" title="Ghanaian people">Ghanaian people</a></div>
