<p>As of 2019, Ghana has a population of 30,083,000.<sup id="cite_ref-176" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-176">&#91;176&#93;</a></sup> Around 29% of the population is under the age of 15, while persons aged 15–64 make up 57.8 percent of the population.<sup id="cite_ref-databank.worldbank.org_177-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-databank.worldbank.org-177">&#91;177&#93;</a></sup> The 2010 census reported that the largest ethnic groups are the Akan (47.3%), the Mole-Dagbani (16.6%), the Ewe (13.9%), the Ga-Dangme (7.4%), the Gurma (5.7%) and the Guan (3.7%).<sup id="cite_ref-statsghana.gov.gh_178-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-statsghana.gov.gh-178">&#91;178&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The median age of Ghanaian citizens is 30 years old and the average household size is 3.6 persons.
</p><p>With <a href="/wiki/Immigration_to_Ghana" title="Immigration to Ghana">recent legal immigration</a> of <a href="/wiki/Skilled_worker" title="Skilled worker">skilled workers</a> who possess <a href="/wiki/Ghana_Card" title="Ghana Card">Ghana Cards</a>, there is a small population of Chinese, Malaysian, Indian, Middle Eastern and European nationals. In 2010, the <a href="/wiki/Ghana_Immigration_Service" title="Ghana Immigration Service">Ghana Immigration Service</a> reported many <a href="/wiki/Economic_migrant" title="Economic migrant">economic migrants</a> and <a href="/wiki/Illegal_immigration_to_Ghana" title="Illegal immigration to Ghana">Illegal immigrants inhabiting Ghana</a>: 14.6% (or 3.1&#160;million) of Ghana's 2010 population (predominantly Nigerians, Burkinabe citizens, Togolese citizens, and Malian citizens). In 1969, under the "Ghana Aliens Compliance Order" enacted by the Prime Minister Kofi Abrefa Busia;<sup id="cite_ref-Ghana_Owes_no_Apology_to_Anybody_for_Aliens_Compliance_Order_179-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghana_Owes_no_Apology_to_Anybody_for_Aliens_Compliance_Order-179">&#91;179&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Border_Guard_Unit" title="Border Guard Unit">Border Guard Unit</a> deported over 3,000,000 aliens and illegal immigrants in three months as they made up 20% of the population at the time.<sup id="cite_ref-Ghana_Owes_no_Apology_to_Anybody_for_Aliens_Compliance_Order_179-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghana_Owes_no_Apology_to_Anybody_for_Aliens_Compliance_Order-179">&#91;179&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-The_History_of_Ghana&#39;s_1969_Aliens_Compliance_Order_180-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-The_History_of_Ghana&#39;s_1969_Aliens_Compliance_Order-180">&#91;180&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-181" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-181">&#91;181&#93;</a></sup> In 2013, there was a mass deportation of illegal miners, more than 4,000 of them Chinese nationals.<sup id="cite_ref-182" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-182">&#91;182&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-183" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-183">&#91;183&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Languages">Languages</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Languages_of_Ghana" title="Languages of Ghana">Languages of Ghana</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Predominant_tribe_in_the_area_-_(Ghana)_LOC_88692692.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/74/Predominant_tribe_in_the_area_-_%28Ghana%29_LOC_88692692.jpg/260px-Predominant_tribe_in_the_area_-_%28Ghana%29_LOC_88692692.jpg" decoding="async" width="260" height="360" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/74/Predominant_tribe_in_the_area_-_%28Ghana%29_LOC_88692692.jpg/390px-Predominant_tribe_in_the_area_-_%28Ghana%29_LOC_88692692.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/74/Predominant_tribe_in_the_area_-_%28Ghana%29_LOC_88692692.jpg/520px-Predominant_tribe_in_the_area_-_%28Ghana%29_LOC_88692692.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3602" data-file-height="4985" /></a><figcaption>A map of Ghana's ethno-linguistic areas.</figcaption></figure>
<p>English is the <a href="/wiki/Official_language" title="Official language">official language</a> of Ghana.<sup id="cite_ref-USA_184-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-USA-184">&#91;184&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-185" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-185">&#91;185&#93;</a></sup> Additionally, there are eleven languages that have the status of government-sponsored languages:
</p>
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/Akan_language" class="mw-redirect" title="Akan language">Akan languages</a> (<a href="/wiki/Asante_dialect" title="Asante dialect">Asante Twi</a>, <a href="/wiki/Akuapem_Twi" class="mw-redirect" title="Akuapem Twi">Akuapem Twi</a>, <a href="/wiki/Fante_dialect" title="Fante dialect">Fante</a> which have a high degree of mutual intelligibility, and <a href="/wiki/Nzema_language" title="Nzema language">Nzema</a>, which is less intelligible with the above)</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Adangme_language" class="mw-redirect" title="Adangme language">Dangme</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Ewe_language" title="Ewe language">Ewe</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Ga_language" title="Ga language">Ga</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Gua_language" title="Gua language">Guan</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Kasem_language" class="mw-redirect" title="Kasem language">Kasem</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Oti-Volta_languages" class="mw-redirect" title="Oti-Volta languages">Mole-Dagbani</a> languages (<a href="/wiki/Dagaare" class="mw-redirect" title="Dagaare">Dagaare</a> and <a href="/wiki/Dagomba_language" class="mw-redirect" title="Dagomba language">Dagbanli</a>)<sup id="cite_ref-NCA_186-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-NCA-186">&#91;186&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-187" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-187">&#91;187&#93;</a></sup></li></ul>
<p>Of these, Asante Twi is the most widely spoken.<sup id="cite_ref-Introduction_To_The_Verbal_and_Multi-Verbalsystem_of_Akan_188-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Introduction_To_The_Verbal_and_Multi-Verbalsystem_of_Akan-188">&#91;188&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Because Ghana is surrounded by <a href="/wiki/List_of_countries_and_territories_where_French_is_an_official_language" title="List of countries and territories where French is an official language">French-speaking countries</a>, French is widely taught in schools and used for commercial and international economic exchanges. Since 2006, Ghana has been an associate member of the <span title="French-language text"><i lang="fr"><a href="/wiki/Organisation_internationale_de_la_Francophonie" title="Organisation internationale de la Francophonie">Organisation internationale de la Francophonie</a></i></span>,<sup id="cite_ref-189" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-189">&#91;189&#93;</a></sup> the global organisation that unites French-speaking countries (84 nations on six continents). In 2005, more than 350,000 Ghanaian children studied French in schools. Since then, its status has been progressively updated to a mandatory language in every junior high school,<sup id="cite_ref-190" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-190">&#91;190&#93;</a></sup> and it is in the process of becoming an official language.<sup id="cite_ref-191" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-191">&#91;191&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-192" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-192">&#91;192&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Ghanaian_Pidgin_English" title="Ghanaian Pidgin English">Ghanaian Pidgin English</a>, also known as Kru English (or in Akan, <i>kroo brofo</i>), is a variety of <a href="/wiki/West_African_Pidgin_English" title="West African Pidgin English">West African Pidgin English</a> spoken in Accra and in the southern towns.<sup id="cite_ref-Huber_1999_p139_193-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Huber_1999_p139-193">&#91;193&#93;</a></sup> It can be divided into two varieties, referred to as "uneducated" or "non-institutionalized" pidgin and "educated" or "institutionalized" pidgin, the former associated with uneducated or illiterate people and the latter acquired and used in institutions such as universities.<sup id="cite_ref-Huber_1999_p138-153_194-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Huber_1999_p138-153-194">&#91;194&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Religion">Religion</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Religion_in_Ghana" title="Religion in Ghana">Religion in Ghana</a></div>
<p><a href="/wiki/Christianity" title="Christianity">Christianity</a> is the largest <b>religion in Ghana</b>, with 71.3% of the population being member of various <a href="/wiki/Christian_denomination" title="Christian denomination">Christian denominations</a> as of 2021 census.<sup id="cite_ref-195" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-195">&#91;195&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Islam" title="Islam">Islam</a> is practised by 19.9% of the total population. According to a 2012 report by <a href="/wiki/Pew_Research" class="mw-redirect" title="Pew Research">Pew Research</a>, 51% of Muslims are followers of <a href="/wiki/Sunni_Islam" title="Sunni Islam">Sunni Islam</a>, while approximately 16% belong to the <a href="/wiki/Ahmadiyya" title="Ahmadiyya">Ahmadiyya</a> movement and around 8% identify with <a href="/wiki/Shia" class="mw-redirect" title="Shia">Shia</a> Islam, while the remainder are <a href="/wiki/Non-denominational_Muslim" title="Non-denominational Muslim">non-denominational Muslims</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-TheWorld&#39;sMuslims:UnityandDiversity_196-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-TheWorld&#39;sMuslims:UnityandDiversity-196">&#91;196&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-rs_197-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-rs-197">&#91;197&#93;</a></sup> There is "no significant link between ethnicity and religion in Ghana".<sup id="cite_ref-stte.gov_198-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-stte.gov-198">&#91;198&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Universal_health_care_and_life_expectancy">Universal health care and life expectancy</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Health_in_Ghana" title="Health in Ghana">Health in Ghana</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/Eye_care_in_Ghana" title="Eye care in Ghana">Eye care in Ghana</a> and <a href="/wiki/Optometry_in_Ghana" title="Optometry in Ghana">Optometry in Ghana</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Life_expectancy_in_Ghana.svg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b9/Life_expectancy_in_Ghana.svg/220px-Life_expectancy_in_Ghana.svg.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="155" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b9/Life_expectancy_in_Ghana.svg/330px-Life_expectancy_in_Ghana.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b9/Life_expectancy_in_Ghana.svg/440px-Life_expectancy_in_Ghana.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="850" data-file-height="600" /></a><figcaption>Development of life expectancy, 1921 to 2019</figcaption></figure>
<p>Ghana has a <a href="/wiki/Universal_health_care" title="Universal health care">universal health care</a> system strictly designated for <a href="/wiki/Ghanaian_people" class="mw-redirect" title="Ghanaian people">Ghanaian nationals</a>, <a href="/wiki/National_Health_Insurance_Scheme_(Ghana)" title="National Health Insurance Scheme (Ghana)">National Health Insurance Scheme</a> (NHIS), is designated for <a href="/wiki/Ghanaian_people" class="mw-redirect" title="Ghanaian people">Ghanaian nationals</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-199" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-199">&#91;199&#93;</a></sup> Health care is variable throughout Ghana and in 2012, over 12&#160;million Ghanaian nationals were covered by the NHIS.<sup id="cite_ref-Ghana:_National_Health_Insurance_Scheme_(NHIS)_200-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghana:_National_Health_Insurance_Scheme_(NHIS)-200">&#91;200&#93;</a></sup> Urban centres are well served and contain most of the hospitals, clinics, and pharmacies. There are over 200 hospitals, and Ghana is a destination for <a href="/wiki/Medical_tourism" title="Medical tourism">medical tourism</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-201" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-201">&#91;201&#93;</a></sup> In 2010, there were 0.1 physicians per 1,000 people and as of 2011<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Ghana&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>, 0.9 hospital beds per 1,000 people.<sup id="cite_ref-databank.worldbank.org_177-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-databank.worldbank.org-177">&#91;177&#93;</a></sup> 5.2% of Ghana's GDP was spent on health in 2010.<sup id="cite_ref-Field_Listing:_Health_expenditures_202-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Field_Listing:_Health_expenditures-202">&#91;202&#93;</a></sup> In 2020, the WHO announced Ghana became the second country in the WHO African Region to attain regulatory system "maturity level 3", the second-highest in the four-tiered WHO classification of <a href="/w/index.php?title=National_medicines_regulatory_systems&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="National medicines regulatory systems (page does not exist)">National medicines regulatory systems</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-203" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-203">&#91;203&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Life expectancy at birth in 2020 was 71 for a female and 65 for a male.<sup id="cite_ref-CDC_204-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CDC-204">&#91;204&#93;</a></sup> In 2013, <a href="/wiki/Infant_mortality" title="Infant mortality">infant mortality</a> was to 39 per 1,000 live births.<sup id="cite_ref-Listing:_Infant_mortality_rate_205-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Listing:_Infant_mortality_rate-205">&#91;205&#93;</a></sup> Sources vary on life expectancy at birth; the <a href="/wiki/World_Health_Organization" title="World Health Organization">World Health Organization</a> (WHO) estimated 62 years for men and 64 years for women born in 2016.<sup id="cite_ref-206" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-206">&#91;206&#93;</a></sup> The fertility rate declined from 3.99 (2000) to 3.28 (2010) with 2.78 in urban region and 3.94 in rural region.<sup id="cite_ref-statsghana.gov.gh_178-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-statsghana.gov.gh-178">&#91;178&#93;</a></sup> The United Nations reports a fertility decline from 6.95 (1970) to 4.82 (2000) to 3.93 live births per woman in 2017.<sup id="cite_ref-207" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-207">&#91;207&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>As of 2012<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Ghana&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>, the <a href="/wiki/HIV/AIDS" title="HIV/AIDS">HIV/AIDS</a> prevalence was estimated at 1.40% among adults aged 15–49.<sup id="cite_ref-208" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-208">&#91;208&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Education">Education</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Education_in_Ghana" title="Education in Ghana">Education in Ghana</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Wikimedia_Outreach_in_Ghana_6.jpeg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2a/Wikimedia_Outreach_in_Ghana_6.jpeg/220px-Wikimedia_Outreach_in_Ghana_6.jpeg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2a/Wikimedia_Outreach_in_Ghana_6.jpeg/330px-Wikimedia_Outreach_in_Ghana_6.jpeg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2a/Wikimedia_Outreach_in_Ghana_6.jpeg/440px-Wikimedia_Outreach_in_Ghana_6.jpeg 2x" data-file-width="2048" data-file-height="1536" /></a><figcaption>Education system's implementation of <a href="/wiki/Information_and_communications_technology" title="Information and communications technology">information and communications technology</a> at the <a href="/wiki/University_of_Ghana" title="University of Ghana">University of Ghana</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>The education system is divided into 3 parts: basic education, secondary cycle, and tertiary education. "Basic education" lasts 11 years (ages 4‒15).<sup id="cite_ref-GEScurr_209-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-GEScurr-209">&#91;209&#93;</a></sup> It is divided into kindergarten (2 years), primary school (2 modules of 3 years) and junior high (3 years). Junior high school ends with the <a href="/wiki/Basic_Education_Certificate_Examination" title="Basic Education Certificate Examination">Basic Education Certificate Examination</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-GEScurr_209-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-GEScurr-209">&#91;209&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-GEScurr2_210-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-GEScurr2-210">&#91;210&#93;</a></sup> Once certified, the pupil can proceed to the secondary cycle.<sup id="cite_ref-BECE_211-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-BECE-211">&#91;211&#93;</a></sup> Hence, the pupil has the choice between general education (offered by the senior high school) and vocational education (offered by the technical senior high school or the technical and vocational institutes). Senior high school lasts 3 years and leads to the <a href="/wiki/West_African_Senior_School_Certificate_Examination" title="West African Senior School Certificate Examination">West African Senior School Certificate Examination</a>, which is a prerequisite for enrollment in a university bachelor's degree programme.<sup id="cite_ref-nab.gov.gh_212-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-nab.gov.gh-212">&#91;212&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Page / location: 7">&#58;&#8202;7&#8202;</span></sup> Polytechnics are open to vocational students.<sup id="cite_ref-UNEVOC_213-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-UNEVOC-213">&#91;213&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>A bachelor's degree requires 4 years of study. It can be followed by a 1- or 2-year master's degree programme, which can be followed by a PhD programme of at least 3 years.<sup id="cite_ref-nab.gov.gh_212-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-nab.gov.gh-212">&#91;212&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Page / location: 9">&#58;&#8202;9&#8202;</span></sup> A polytechnic programme lasts 2 or 3 years.<sup id="cite_ref-UNEVOC_213-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-UNEVOC-213">&#91;213&#93;</a></sup> Ghana possesses colleges of education.<sup id="cite_ref-214" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-214">&#91;214&#93;</a></sup> Some of the universities are the <a href="/wiki/University_of_Ghana" title="University of Ghana">University of Ghana</a>, <a href="/wiki/Kwame_Nkrumah_University_of_Science_and_Technology" title="Kwame Nkrumah University of Science and Technology">Kwame Nkrumah University of Science and Technology</a>, and <a href="/wiki/University_of_Cape_Coast" title="University of Cape Coast">University of Cape Coast</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-215" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-215">&#91;215&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>There are over 95% of children in school.<sup id="cite_ref-UNICEF_–_Basic_Education_and_Gender_Equality_216-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-UNICEF_–_Basic_Education_and_Gender_Equality-216">&#91;216&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-217" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-217">&#91;217&#93;</a></sup> The female and male ages 15–24 years literacy rate was 81% in 2010, with males at 82%,<sup id="cite_ref-218" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-218">&#91;218&#93;</a></sup> and females at 80%.<sup id="cite_ref-219" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-219">&#91;219&#93;</a></sup> A education system annually attracts <a href="/wiki/Foreign_student" class="mw-redirect" title="Foreign student">foreign students</a> particularly in the university sector.<sup id="cite_ref-220" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-220">&#91;220&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-221" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-221">&#91;221&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Ghana has a <a href="/wiki/Free_education" title="Free education">free education</a> 6-year primary school education system beginning at age 6.<sup id="cite_ref-Ghana_Lauded_for_Free_Primary_School_Program_222-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghana_Lauded_for_Free_Primary_School_Program-222">&#91;222&#93;</a></sup> The government largely funds basic education comprising public primary schools and public junior high schools. Senior high schools were subsidised by the government until September 2017/2018 academic year that senior high education became free.<sup id="cite_ref-223" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-223">&#91;223&#93;</a></sup> At the higher education level, the government funds more than 80% of resources provided to public universities, polytechnics and teacher training colleges. As part of the Free Compulsory Universal Basic Education, Fcube, the government supplies all basic education schools with all their textbooks and other educational supplies, like exercise books. Senior high schools are provided with all their textbook requirements by the government. Private schools acquire their educational material from private suppliers.<sup id="cite_ref-KoinzerNikolai2017_224-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-KoinzerNikolai2017-224">&#91;224&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Culture">Culture</span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:FB_20151104_19_47_22_Saved_Picture(1).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/69/FB_20151104_19_47_22_Saved_Picture%281%29.jpg/220px-FB_20151104_19_47_22_Saved_Picture%281%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/69/FB_20151104_19_47_22_Saved_Picture%281%29.jpg/330px-FB_20151104_19_47_22_Saved_Picture%281%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/69/FB_20151104_19_47_22_Saved_Picture%281%29.jpg/440px-FB_20151104_19_47_22_Saved_Picture%281%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="720" data-file-height="540" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Hogbetsotso_festival" title="Hogbetsotso festival">Hogbetsotso festival</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Volta_region" class="mw-redirect" title="Volta region">Volta region</a></figcaption></figure><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Culture_of_Ghana" title="Culture of Ghana">Culture of Ghana</a></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Food_and_drink">Food and drink</span></h3>
<p><a href="/wiki/Ghanaian_cuisine" title="Ghanaian cuisine">Ghanaian cuisine</a> includes an assortment of soups and stews with varied seafoods; most Ghanaian soups are prepared with vegetables, meat, poultry or fish.<sup id="cite_ref-Ghanaian_cuisine,_banku,_okra_and_soup_225-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghanaian_cuisine,_banku,_okra_and_soup-225">&#91;225&#93;</a></sup> Fish is important in the diet with tilapia, roasted and fried <a href="/wiki/Whitebait" title="Whitebait">whitebait</a>, smoked fish and crayfish, all being common components of Ghanaian dishes.<sup id="cite_ref-Ghanaian_cuisine,_banku,_okra_and_soup_225-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghanaian_cuisine,_banku,_okra_and_soup-225">&#91;225&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Banku_(dish)" title="Banku (dish)">Banku</a> (akple) is a common starchy food made from ground corn (maize),<sup id="cite_ref-Ghanaian_cuisine,_banku,_okra_and_soup_225-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghanaian_cuisine,_banku,_okra_and_soup-225">&#91;225&#93;</a></sup> and cornmeal based staples kɔmi (<a href="/wiki/Kenkey" title="Kenkey">kenkey</a>) and banku (akple) are usually accompanied by some form of fried fish (chinam) or grilled tilapia and a very spicy condiment made from raw red and green chillies, onions and tomatoes (pepper sauce).<sup id="cite_ref-Ghanaian_cuisine,_banku,_okra_and_soup_225-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghanaian_cuisine,_banku,_okra_and_soup-225">&#91;225&#93;</a></sup> Banku and tilapia is a combo served in most restaurants.<sup id="cite_ref-Ghanaian_cuisine,_banku,_okra_and_soup_225-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghanaian_cuisine,_banku,_okra_and_soup-225">&#91;225&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Fufu" title="Fufu">Fufu</a> is the most common exported Ghanaian dish and is a delicacy across the <a href="/wiki/African_diaspora" title="African diaspora">African diaspora</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Ghanaian_cuisine,_banku,_okra_and_soup_225-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghanaian_cuisine,_banku,_okra_and_soup-225">&#91;225&#93;</a></sup> Rice is an established staple meal across the country, with various rice based dishes serving as breakfast, lunch and dinner, the main variants are waakye, plain rice and stew (eight kontomire or tomato gravy), fried rice and <a href="/wiki/Jollof_rice" title="Jollof rice">jollof rice</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-226" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-226">&#91;226&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Literature">Literature</span></h3>
<div class="excerpt-block"><style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1066933788">.mw-parser-output .excerpt-hat .mw-editsection-like{font-style:normal}</style><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable dablink excerpt-hat selfref">This section is an excerpt from <a href="/wiki/Ghanaian_literature" title="Ghanaian literature">Ghanaian literature</a>.<span class="mw-editsection-like plainlinks"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Ghanaian_literature&amp;action=edit">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div><div class="excerpt">
<p><a href="/wiki/Ghanaian_literature" title="Ghanaian literature">Ghanaian literature</a> is literature produced by authors from Ghana or in the <a href="/wiki/Ghanaians" title="Ghanaians">Ghanaian diaspora</a>. The tradition of literature starts with a long oral tradition, was influence heavily by western literature during colonial rule, and became prominent with a post-colonial nationalist tradition in the mid 20th century.<sup id="cite_ref-227" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-227">&#91;227&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Ghanaian_literature_:0_228-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghanaian_literature_:0-228">&#91;228&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Ghanaian_literature_:1_229-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghanaian_literature_:1-229">&#91;229&#93;</a></sup> The current literary community continues with a diverse network of voices both within and outside the country today, including film, theatre, and modern digital formats such as blogging.<sup id="cite_ref-Ghanaian_literature_:0_228-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghanaian_literature_:0-228">&#91;228&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Ghanaian_literature_:1_229-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghanaian_literature_:1-229">&#91;229&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The most prominent authors are novelists <a href="/wiki/J._E._Casely_Hayford" title="J. E. Casely Hayford">J. E. Casely Hayford</a>, <a href="/wiki/Ayi_Kwei_Armah" title="Ayi Kwei Armah">Ayi Kwei Armah</a> and <a href="/wiki/Nii_Ayikwei_Parkes" class="mw-redirect" title="Nii Ayikwei Parkes">Nii Ayikwei Parkes</a>, who gained international acclaim with the books <i><a href="/wiki/Ethiopia_Unbound" title="Ethiopia Unbound">Ethiopia Unbound</a></i> (1911), <i><a href="/wiki/The_Beautyful_Ones_Are_Not_Yet_Born" title="The Beautyful Ones Are Not Yet Born">The Beautyful Ones Are Not Yet Born</a></i> (1968) and <i>Tail of the Blue Bird</i> (2009), respectively.<sup id="cite_ref-Ghanaian_literature_LIT_230-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghanaian_literature_LIT-230">&#91;230&#93;</a></sup> In addition to novels, other literature arts such as theatre and poetry have also had a very good development and support at the national level with prominent playwrights and poets <a href="/wiki/Joe_de_Graft" title="Joe de Graft">Joe de Graft</a> and <a href="/wiki/Efua_Sutherland" title="Efua Sutherland">Efua Sutherland</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Ghanaian_literature_LIT_230-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghanaian_literature_LIT-230">&#91;230&#93;</a></sup> 
</p>
The Ghanaian national literature radio programme and accompanying publication <i><a href="/wiki/Voices_of_Ghana" title="Voices of Ghana">Voices of Ghana</a> (1955-1957)</i> was one of the earliest on the African continent, and helped establish the  scope of the contemporary literary tradition in Ghana.<sup id="cite_ref-231" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-231">&#91;231&#93;</a></sup> Scholarship of Anglo-phone Africa sometimes favors literatures from other geographies, such as the <a href="/wiki/Nigerian_literature" title="Nigerian literature">literature of Nigeria</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-232" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-232">&#91;232&#93;</a></sup></div></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Clothing">Clothing</span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Adinkra_motifs_Rattray_1927.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2a/Adinkra_motifs_Rattray_1927.jpg/220px-Adinkra_motifs_Rattray_1927.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="121" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2a/Adinkra_motifs_Rattray_1927.jpg/330px-Adinkra_motifs_Rattray_1927.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/2/2a/Adinkra_motifs_Rattray_1927.jpg 2x" data-file-width="390" data-file-height="214" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Adinkra_symbols" title="Adinkra symbols">Adinkra symbols</a> by <a href="/wiki/Robert_Sutherland_Rattray" title="Robert Sutherland Rattray">Robert Sutherland Rattray</a></figcaption></figure><p>
During the 13th century, Ghanaians developed their unique art of <i><a href="/wiki/Adinkra_symbols" title="Adinkra symbols">adinkra</a></i> printing. Hand-printed and hand-embroidered adinkra clothes were made and used exclusively by royalty for devotional ceremonies. Each of the motifs that make up the <a href="/wiki/Text_corpus" title="Text corpus">corpus</a> of adinkra symbolism has a name and meaning derived from a proverb, a historical event, human attitude, <a href="/wiki/Ethology" title="Ethology">ethology</a>, <a href="/wiki/Plant_life-form" title="Plant life-form">plant life-form</a>, or shapes of inanimate and man-made objects. The meanings of the motifs may be categorised into aesthetics, ethics, human relations, and concepts.<sup id="cite_ref-LIT_233-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-LIT-233">&#91;233&#93;</a></sup> The Adinkra symbols have a decorative function as tattoos but also represent objects that encapsulate evocative messages that convey traditional wisdom, aspects of life, or the environment. There are many symbols with distinct meanings, often linked with proverbs. In the words of <a href="/wiki/Kwame_Anthony_Appiah" title="Kwame Anthony Appiah">Anthony Appiah</a>, they were one of the means in a pre-literate society for "supporting the transmission of a complex and nuanced body of practice and belief".<sup id="cite_ref-234" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-234">&#91;234&#93;</a></sup></p><figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Kent_wove.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5b/Kent_wove.jpg/170px-Kent_wove.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="235" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5b/Kent_wove.jpg/255px-Kent_wove.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5b/Kent_wove.jpg/340px-Kent_wove.jpg 2x" data-file-width="371" data-file-height="513" /></a><figcaption>Kente cloth, the traditional or national cloth of Ghana, is worn by most southern Ghanaian ethnic groups, including the <a href="/wiki/Akan_people" title="Akan people">Akan</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Ga-Adangbe_people" title="Ga-Adangbe people">Ga</a>, and the <a href="/wiki/Ewe_people" title="Ewe people">Ewe</a>.</figcaption></figure>
<p>Along with the <i>adinkra cloth,</i> Ghanaians use many cloth fabrics for their traditional attire.<sup id="cite_ref-Ghanaian_Kente_Cloth_235-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghanaian_Kente_Cloth-235">&#91;235&#93;</a></sup> The different ethnic groups have their own individual cloth. The most well known is the <a href="/wiki/Kente_cloth" title="Kente cloth">Kente cloth</a><sup id="cite_ref-Ghanaian_Kente_Cloth_235-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghanaian_Kente_Cloth-235">&#91;235&#93;</a></sup> Kente is a very important national costume and clothing, and these clothes are used to make traditional and modern Kente attire.<sup id="cite_ref-Ghanaian_Kente_Cloth_235-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghanaian_Kente_Cloth-235">&#91;235&#93;</a></sup> Different symbols and different colours mean different things.<sup id="cite_ref-Ghanaian_Kente_Cloth_235-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghanaian_Kente_Cloth-235">&#91;235&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Kente_cloth" title="Kente cloth">Kente</a> is the most famous of all the Ghanaian clothes.<sup id="cite_ref-Ghanaian_Kente_Cloth_235-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghanaian_Kente_Cloth-235">&#91;235&#93;</a></sup> Kente is a ceremonial cloth hand-woven on a horizontal <a href="/wiki/Loom" title="Loom">treadle loom</a> and strips measuring about 4&#160;inches wide are sewn together into larger pieces of cloths.<sup id="cite_ref-Ghanaian_Kente_Cloth_235-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghanaian_Kente_Cloth-235">&#91;235&#93;</a></sup> Cloths come in various colours, sizes and designs and are worn during very important social and religious occasions.<sup id="cite_ref-Ghanaian_Kente_Cloth_235-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghanaian_Kente_Cloth-235">&#91;235&#93;</a></sup> In a cultural context, kente is more important than just a cloth as it is a visual representation of history and also a form of written language through weaving.<sup id="cite_ref-Ghanaian_Kente_Cloth_235-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghanaian_Kente_Cloth-235">&#91;235&#93;</a></sup> The term kente has its roots in the Akan word <i>kɛntɛn</i> which means a basket and the first kente weavers used raffia fibres to weave cloths that looked like kenten (a basket); and thus were referred to as <i>kenten ntoma</i>; meaning basket cloth.<sup id="cite_ref-Ghanaian_Kente_Cloth_235-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghanaian_Kente_Cloth-235">&#91;235&#93;</a></sup> The original Akan name of the cloth was <i>nsaduaso</i> or <i>nwontoma</i>, meaning "a cloth hand-woven on a loom"; however, "kente" is the most frequently used term today.
Kente is also woven by the Ewe people (Ewe Kente) in the Volta Region. The main weaving centers are Agortime area and Agbozume. Agbozume has a vibrant kente market attracting patrons from all over west Africa and the diaspora.<sup id="cite_ref-Ghanaian_Kente_Cloth_235-9" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghanaian_Kente_Cloth-235">&#91;235&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1096954695/mw-parser-output/.tmulti"><div class="thumb tmulti tleft"><div class="thumbinner multiimageinner" style="width:308px;max-width:308px"><div class="trow"><div class="tsingle" style="width:152px;max-width:152px"><div class="thumbimage"><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:Comtemporary_Ghanaian_men_fashion.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/b/b4/Comtemporary_Ghanaian_men_fashion.jpg/150px-Comtemporary_Ghanaian_men_fashion.jpg" decoding="async" width="150" height="150" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/b/b4/Comtemporary_Ghanaian_men_fashion.jpg/225px-Comtemporary_Ghanaian_men_fashion.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/b/b4/Comtemporary_Ghanaian_men_fashion.jpg/300px-Comtemporary_Ghanaian_men_fashion.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2000" data-file-height="2000" /></a></span></div><div class="thumbcaption">Contemporary Ghanaian men's fashion with Kente and other traditional styles</div></div><div class="tsingle" style="width:152px;max-width:152px"><div class="thumbimage"><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:Comtemporary_Ghanaian_womenswear.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/1/17/Comtemporary_Ghanaian_womenswear.jpg/150px-Comtemporary_Ghanaian_womenswear.jpg" decoding="async" width="150" height="150" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/1/17/Comtemporary_Ghanaian_womenswear.jpg/225px-Comtemporary_Ghanaian_womenswear.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/1/17/Comtemporary_Ghanaian_womenswear.jpg/300px-Comtemporary_Ghanaian_womenswear.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2000" data-file-height="2000" /></a></span></div><div class="thumbcaption">Contemporary Ghanaian women's fashion with African print/Ankara and other fabrics</div></div></div></div></div>
<p>Contemporary Ghanaian fashion includes traditional and modern styles and fabrics and has made its way into the African and global fashion scene. The cloth known as <a href="/wiki/African_wax_prints" title="African wax prints">African print fabric</a> was created out of Dutch wax textiles. It is believed that in the late 19th century, Dutch ships on their way to Asia stocked with machine-made textiles that mimicked Indonesian <a href="/wiki/Batik" title="Batik">batik</a> stopped at many West African ports on the way. The fabrics did not do well in Asia. However, in West Africa—mainly Ghana where there was an already established market for cloths and textiles—the client base grew and it was changed to include local and traditional designs, colours and patterns to cater to the taste of the new consumers.<sup id="cite_ref-236" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-236">&#91;236&#93;</a></sup> Today outside of Africa it is called "Ankara," and it has a client base well beyond Ghana and Africa as a whole. It is popular among Caribbean peoples and African Americans; celebrities such as <a href="/wiki/Solange_Knowles" title="Solange Knowles">Solange Knowles</a> and her sister <a href="/wiki/Beyonc%C3%A9" title="Beyoncé">Beyoncé</a> have been seen wearing African print attire.<sup id="cite_ref-237" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-237">&#91;237&#93;</a></sup> Many designers from countries in North America and Europe are now using African prints, and they have gained a global interest.<sup id="cite_ref-238" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-238">&#91;238&#93;</a></sup> British luxury fashion house <a href="/wiki/Burberry" title="Burberry">Burberry</a> created a collection around Ghanaian styles.<sup id="cite_ref-239" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-239">&#91;239&#93;</a></sup> American musician <a href="/wiki/Gwen_Stefani" title="Gwen Stefani">Gwen Stefani</a> has repeatedly incorporated African prints into her clothing line and can often be seen wearing it.<sup id="cite_ref-240" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-240">&#91;240&#93;</a></sup> Internationally acclaimed Ghanaian-British designer <a href="/wiki/Ozwald_Boateng" title="Ozwald Boateng">Ozwald Boateng</a> introduced African print suits in his 2012 collection.<sup id="cite_ref-241" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-241">&#91;241&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Music_and_dance">Music and dance</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Azonto" title="Azonto">Azonto</a> and <a href="/wiki/Kpanlogo" title="Kpanlogo">Kpanlogo</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><span><video id="mwe_player_2" poster="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/85/Traditional_Adowa_dance_form_and_music_performance.ogv/220px--Traditional_Adowa_dance_form_and_music_performance.ogv.jpg" controls="" preload="none" class="mw-file-element" width="220" height="293" data-durationhint="16" data-mwtitle="Traditional_Adowa_dance_form_and_music_performance.ogv" data-mwprovider="wikimediacommons" resource="/wiki/File:Traditional_Adowa_dance_form_and_music_performance.ogv"><source src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/transcoded/8/85/Traditional_Adowa_dance_form_and_music_performance.ogv/Traditional_Adowa_dance_form_and_music_performance.ogv.480p.webm" type="video/webm; <p>Music incorporates types of musical instruments such as the talking drum ensembles, <a href="/wiki/Akan_Drum" title="Akan Drum">Akan Drum</a>, <a href="/wiki/Goje" title="Goje">goje</a> fiddle and koloko lute, court music, including the Akan <a href="/wiki/Seperewa" title="Seperewa">Seperewa</a>, the Akan atumpan, the Ga kpanlogo styles, and log <a href="/wiki/Xylophone" title="Xylophone">xylophones</a> used in asonko music.<sup id="cite_ref-Ghana:_From_Highlife_to_Hiplife_242-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghana:_From_Highlife_to_Hiplife-242">&#91;242&#93;</a></sup> African jazz was created by <a href="/wiki/Guy_Warren" title="Guy Warren">Kofi Ghanaba</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-243" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-243">&#91;243&#93;</a></sup> A form of secular music is <a href="/wiki/Highlife" title="Highlife">highlife</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Ghana:_From_Highlife_to_Hiplife_242-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghana:_From_Highlife_to_Hiplife-242">&#91;242&#93;</a></sup> Highlife originated in the 19th and 20th centuries and spread throughout West Africa.<sup id="cite_ref-Ghana:_From_Highlife_to_Hiplife_242-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghana:_From_Highlife_to_Hiplife-242">&#91;242&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In the 1990s, a genre of music was created incorporating the influences of highlife, Afro-reggae, <a href="/wiki/Dancehall" title="Dancehall">dancehall</a> and <a href="/wiki/Hip_hop_music" title="Hip hop music">hip hop</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Ghana:_From_Highlife_to_Hiplife_242-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghana:_From_Highlife_to_Hiplife-242">&#91;242&#93;</a></sup> This hybrid was called <a href="/wiki/Hiplife" title="Hiplife">hiplife</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Ghana:_From_Highlife_to_Hiplife_242-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghana:_From_Highlife_to_Hiplife-242">&#91;242&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>There are dances for occasions.<sup id="cite_ref-Dance,_Ghana_244-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Dance,_Ghana-244">&#91;244&#93;</a></sup> Dances for celebrations include the <a href="/wiki/Adowa_dance" title="Adowa dance">Adowa</a>, <a href="/wiki/Kpanlogo" title="Kpanlogo">Kpanlogo</a>, <a href="/wiki/Azonto" title="Azonto">Azonto</a>, <a href="/w/index.php?title=Klama&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Klama (page does not exist)">Klama</a>, <a href="/wiki/Agbadza" title="Agbadza">Agbadza</a>, <a href="/wiki/Borborbor" title="Borborbor">Borborbor</a> and <a href="/w/index.php?title=Bamaya&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Bamaya (page does not exist)">Bamaya</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Dance,_Ghana_244-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Dance,_Ghana-244">&#91;244&#93;</a></sup> The Nana Otafrija Pallbearing Services, also known as the <a href="/wiki/Dancing_Pallbearers" title="Dancing Pallbearers">Dancing Pallbearers</a>, come from the coastal town of <a href="/wiki/Prampram" title="Prampram">Prampram</a>. The group was featured in a BBC feature story in 2017, and footage from the story became part of an Internet meme in the wake of the <a href="/wiki/COVID-19_pandemic" title="COVID-19 pandemic">COVID-19 world pandemic</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-245" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-245">&#91;245&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Media">Media</span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Ghana_Trustworthiness_of_Media.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5e/Ghana_Trustworthiness_of_Media.jpg/220px-Ghana_Trustworthiness_of_Media.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="153" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5e/Ghana_Trustworthiness_of_Media.jpg/330px-Ghana_Trustworthiness_of_Media.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5e/Ghana_Trustworthiness_of_Media.jpg/440px-Ghana_Trustworthiness_of_Media.jpg 2x" data-file-width="445" data-file-height="310" /></a><figcaption>Mass media, news and information provided by television.</figcaption></figure>
<p>Chapter 12 of the 1992 Constitution of Ghana guarantees <a href="/wiki/Freedom_of_the_press" title="Freedom of the press">freedom of the press</a> and independence of the media, while Chapter 2 prohibits censorship.<sup id="cite_ref-gov_246-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-gov-246">&#91;246&#93;</a></sup> Post-independence, private outlets closed during the military governments, and media laws prevented criticism of government.<sup id="cite_ref-Anokwa_247-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Anokwa-247">&#91;247&#93;</a></sup> Press freedoms were restored in 1992, and after the election in 2000 of Kufuor, the tensions between the private media and government decreased. Kufuor supported press freedom and repealed a <a href="/wiki/Defamation" title="Defamation">libel</a> law, and maintained that the media had to act responsibly.<sup id="cite_ref-pr_248-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-pr-248">&#91;248&#93;</a></sup> The media have been described as "one of the most unfettered" in Africa.<sup id="cite_ref-bbc_249-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bbc-249">&#91;249&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1948, the Gold Coast Film Unit was set up in the Information Services Department.<sup id="cite_ref-250" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-250">&#91;250&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Architecture">Architecture</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Ghana%27s_material_cultural_heritage" title="Ghana&#39;s material cultural heritage">Ghana's material cultural heritage</a> and <a href="/wiki/List_of_museums_in_Ghana" title="List of museums in Ghana">Ghanaian museums</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:A_drone_footage_of_Accra_central,_Ghana.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b6/A_drone_footage_of_Accra_central%2C_Ghana.jpg/220px-A_drone_footage_of_Accra_central%2C_Ghana.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="133" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b6/A_drone_footage_of_Accra_central%2C_Ghana.jpg/330px-A_drone_footage_of_Accra_central%2C_Ghana.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b6/A_drone_footage_of_Accra_central%2C_Ghana.jpg/440px-A_drone_footage_of_Accra_central%2C_Ghana.jpg 2x" data-file-width="960" data-file-height="579" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/High-rise_building" class="mw-redirect" title="High-rise building">High-rise buildings</a> in <a href="/wiki/Accra" title="Accra">Accra</a>, the capital</figcaption></figure>
<p>There are 2 types of construction: the series of adjacent buildings in an enclosure around a common, and the round huts with grass roof.<sup id="cite_ref-ARC_251-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ARC-251">&#91;251&#93;</a></sup> The round huts with grass roof architecture are situated in the northern regions, while the series of adjacent buildings are in the southern regions. <a href="/wiki/Postmodern_architecture" title="Postmodern architecture">Postmodern architecture</a> and <a href="/wiki/High-tech_architecture" title="High-tech architecture">high-tech architecture</a> buildings are in the southern regions, while heritage sites are evident in the more than 30 forts and castles in the country, such as <a href="/wiki/Fort_William,_Ghana" title="Fort William, Ghana">Fort William</a> and <a href="/wiki/Fort_Amsterdam,_Ghana" title="Fort Amsterdam, Ghana">Fort Amsterdam</a>. Ghana has museums that are situated inside castles, and 2 are situated inside a fort.<sup id="cite_ref-Museums_and_Monuments_Board_252-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Museums_and_Monuments_Board-252">&#91;252&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Armed_Forces_Museum_(Ghana)" title="Armed Forces Museum (Ghana)">Military Museum</a> and the <a href="/wiki/National_Museum_of_Ghana" title="National Museum of Ghana">National Museum</a> organise temporary exhibitions.<sup id="cite_ref-Museums_and_Monuments_Board_252-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Museums_and_Monuments_Board-252">&#91;252&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Ghana has museums that show an in-depth look at specific regions. There are a number of museums that provide insight into the traditions and history of the geographical areas.<sup id="cite_ref-Museums_and_Monuments_Board_252-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Museums_and_Monuments_Board-252">&#91;252&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Cape_Coast_Castle" title="Cape Coast Castle">Cape Coast Castle</a> Museum and St. Georges Castle (<a href="/wiki/Elmina_Castle" title="Elmina Castle">Elmina Castle</a>) Museum offer guided tours. The <a href="/wiki/Kwame_Nkrumah_University_of_Science_and_Technology" title="Kwame Nkrumah University of Science and Technology">Museum of Science and Technology</a> provides its visitors with a look into the domain of scientific development, through exhibits of objects of scientific and technological interest.<sup id="cite_ref-Museums_and_Monuments_Board_252-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Museums_and_Monuments_Board-252">&#91;252&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Sports">Sports</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Sports_in_Ghana" title="Sports in Ghana">Sports in Ghana</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:2010_Opening_Ceremony_-_Ghana_entering.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/66/2010_Opening_Ceremony_-_Ghana_entering.jpg/170px-2010_Opening_Ceremony_-_Ghana_entering.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="170" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/66/2010_Opening_Ceremony_-_Ghana_entering.jpg/255px-2010_Opening_Ceremony_-_Ghana_entering.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/66/2010_Opening_Ceremony_-_Ghana_entering.jpg/340px-2010_Opening_Ceremony_-_Ghana_entering.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1207" data-file-height="1206" /></a><figcaption>Ghanaian <a href="/wiki/Winter_sport" class="mw-redirect" title="Winter sport">winter sports</a> Olympic team at the <a href="/wiki/Opening_ceremony" title="Opening ceremony">opening ceremony</a> of the 2010 <a href="/wiki/Winter_Olympics" class="mw-redirect" title="Winter Olympics">Winter Olympics</a></figcaption></figure>
<p><a href="/wiki/Association_football" title="Association football">Association football</a> is the top spectator sport in Ghana.<sup id="cite_ref-Ghana_thrilled_by_historic_title_253-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghana_thrilled_by_historic_title-253">&#91;253&#93;</a></sup> Ghana has won the <a href="/wiki/Africa_Cup_of_Nations" title="Africa Cup of Nations">Africa Cup of Nations</a> four times, the <a href="/wiki/FIFA_U-20_World_Cup" title="FIFA U-20 World Cup">FIFA U-20 World Cup</a> once, and has participated in three consecutive <a href="/wiki/FIFA_World_Cup" title="FIFA World Cup">FIFA World Cups</a> in 2006, 2010, and 2014.<sup id="cite_ref-Ghana_thrilled_by_historic_title_253-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ghana_thrilled_by_historic_title-253">&#91;253&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/International_Federation_of_Football_History_%26_Statistics" title="International Federation of Football History &amp; Statistics">International Federation of Football History and Statistics</a> crowned Asante Kotoko SC as the <a href="/wiki/International_Federation_of_Football_History_%26_Statistics#Continental_Clubs_of_the_20th_century" title="International Federation of Football History &amp; Statistics">African club of the 20th century</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Africa&#39;s_club_of_the_Century_254-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Africa&#39;s_club_of_the_Century-254">&#91;254&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>
Ghana competes in the <a href="/wiki/Commonwealth_Games" title="Commonwealth Games">Commonwealth Games</a>, sending athletes in every edition since <a href="/wiki/1954_British_Empire_and_Commonwealth_Games" title="1954 British Empire and Commonwealth Games">1954</a> (except for the <a href="/wiki/1986_Commonwealth_Games" title="1986 Commonwealth Games">1986 games</a>). Ghana has won 57 medals at the Commonwealth Games, including 15 gold, with all but one of their medals coming in athletics and boxing. The country has also produced a number of boxers, including <a href="/wiki/Azumah_Nelson" title="Azumah Nelson">Azumah Nelson</a> a three-time world champion,<sup id="cite_ref-255" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-255">&#91;255&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Top_5_Ghanaian_Boxers_256-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Top_5_Ghanaian_Boxers-256">&#91;256&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Nana_Konadu" title="Nana Konadu">Nana Yaw Konadu</a> also a three-time world champion,<sup id="cite_ref-Top_5_Ghanaian_Boxers_256-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Top_5_Ghanaian_Boxers-256">&#91;256&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Ike_Quartey" title="Ike Quartey">Ike Quartey</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-Top_5_Ghanaian_Boxers_256-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Top_5_Ghanaian_Boxers-256">&#91;256&#93;</a></sup> and <a href="/wiki/Joshua_Clottey" title="Joshua Clottey">Joshua Clottey</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Top_5_Ghanaian_Boxers_256-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Top_5_Ghanaian_Boxers-256">&#91;256&#93;</a></sup></p><div style="clear:both;"></div>

<p><b><a href="/wiki/Geographic_coordinate_system" title="Geographic coordinate system">Lat. <small>and</small> Long.</a> <span class="geo-inline"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1156832818"><span class="plainlinks nourlexpansion"><a class="external text" href="https://geohack.toolforge.org/geohack.php?pagename=Ghana&amp;params=05_33_18_N_00_11_33_W_"><span class="geo-default"><span class="geo-dms" title="Maps, aerial photos, and other data for this location"><span class="latitude">05°33′18″N</span> <span class="longitude">00°11′33″W</span></span></span><span class="geo-multi-punct">&#xfeff; / &#xfeff;</span><span class="geo-nondefault"><span class="geo-dec" title="Maps, aerial photos, and other data for this location">5.55500°N 0.19250°W</span><span style="display:none">&#xfeff; / <span class="geo">5.55500; -0.19250</span></span></span></a></span></span> <span style="color:darkblue;">(Accra)</span></b><br /><b><a href="/wiki/Geographic_coordinate_system" title="Geographic coordinate system">Lat. <small>and</small> Long.</a> <span class="geo-inline"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1156832818"><span class="plainlinks nourlexpansion"><a class="external text" href="https://geohack.toolforge.org/geohack.php?pagename=Ghana&amp;params=06_42_00_N_01_37_30_W_"><span class="geo-default"><span class="geo-dms" title="Maps, aerial photos, and other data for this location"><span class="latitude">06°42′00″N</span> <span class="longitude">01°37′30″W</span></span></span><span class="geo-multi-punct">&#xfeff; / &#xfeff;</span><span class="geo-nondefault"><span class="geo-dec" title="Maps, aerial photos, and other data for this location">6.70000°N 1.62500°W</span><span style="display:none">&#xfeff; / <span class="geo">6.70000; -1.62500</span></span></span></a></span></span> <span style="color:darkblue;">(Kumasi)</span></b>
